### 1. [Textile organic electrochemical transistors in biosensing and bioelectronics including materials architectures and emerging applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/107a171b12b8ab9e5a62edbc89eb265aa4b8b19c)

**Authors:** R. Brendgen, A. Schwarz-Pfeiffer  
**Venue/category:** Discover Electrochemistry  
**Date:** 2026-10-16  
**DOI:** `10.1007/s44373-026-00166-7`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 78  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, wearable bioelectronics, electrophysiological, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) have emerged as promising platforms for wearable bioelectronics due to their low-voltage operation, intrinsic signal amplification, and efficient coupling of ionic and electronic charge transport. Although textile OECTs have attracted increasing attention for health monitoring and smart textile applications, existing reviews have primarily focused on fibre-based devices and do not capture the full diversity of textile OECT architectures. To address this gap, this review systematically evaluates the current state of textile OECT technology and its development toward wearable bioelectronic systems. The review covers OECT operating principles, materials, textile-compatible fabrication approaches, textile device architectures, and emerging applications in biochemical and electrophysiological sensing. The literature reveals a rapidly expanding field encompassing fibre- and yarn-based OECTs, printed textile devices, and emerging stacked and vertical architectures, alongside significant advances in sensing performance and textile integration. These developments demonstrate the considerable potential of textile OECTs as multifunctional platforms for continuous and unobtrusive physiological monitoring. Future progress will depend on overcoming challenges related to long-term stability, washability, scalable manufacturing, and standardised performance evaluation under realistic textile operating conditions.

### 3. [Architecting Organic Electrochemical Transistors for Bioelectronics: From Channel Micro‐Engineering to Microarray Systems](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/d95c994bf04d0bcb0454e7f5e20c133f42560d9f)

**Authors:** Mahroza Kanwal Khan, Ying-Hui Zhong, Yi-Fei He, Wan Yue, Yao-Kang Zhang, Zhuang Xie, Xue-Chang Zhou  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Functional Materials  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1002/adfm.78981`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 72  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, biointerface, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT Organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) have emerged as transformative devices that revolutionize next‐generation electronics to bridge ionic/electronic signaling at the interface between biological and microelectronic systems. This review systematically explores fundamental breakthroughs in OECT channel material engineering and device manufacturing across micro/nanoscales for emerging bioelectronic systems, emphasizing microarchitecture design and patterning strategies that unlock advantages including enhanced transistor performance, high speed, improved mechanical durability, high‐fidelity integration, as well as biocompatibility. We first briefly introduce the device physics underpinning OECTs and state‐of‐the‐art material innovations, focusing on nanostructure‐dominated mixed ionic–electronic conduction. Subsequently, we elaborate on tailored microscale channel architectures, encompassing 1D fibers, 2D micro/nanoporous films, and 3D soft hydrogel networks to address both electronic and mechanical challenges. Furthermore, we highlight advances in scalable fabrication techniques such as printing/photolithography‐based approaches and vertical architectures for developing high‐density flexible OECT arrays. In addition, diverse bioelectronic applications are summarized, including biosensing, signal processing, implantable biointerfaces, and brain‐inspired computing, demonstrating the versatility of OECTs. The discussion concludes by outlining future directions to enhance device reliability and scalability, addressing critical challenges such as material stability and integration complexity. Through this comprehensive analysis, we provide a roadmap for advancing OECT micro/nanotechnology, inspiring future research and accelerating its adoption in bioelectronics.

### 4. [Scalable Patterning of Organic Electrochemical Transistors for Logic Gates and Neuromorphic Computing.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/700a8b65971dcdd78d6fa6aa3603a267d06135cf)

**Authors:** Yi-Fan Xie, Jun-Yang Deng, Cheng-Qi Wei, Jia-Hao Zhao, Liu-Yuan Lan, Ya-Zhuo Kuang, Miao Cheng, Chang-Rui Liu, Yue Tian, Han-Lin Wang, Wan Yue, Jian Liu et al.  
**Venue/category:** Small  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1002/smll.76279`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 71  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, biointerface, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor, brain-computer interface  

**Abstract**

Organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) are promising building blocks for bioelectronics owing to their efficient ion-electron coupling, soft-material compatibility and high transconductance. However, their large off-state currents and poor scalability have so far limited high-density integration and circuit-level functionality. Here we introduce a patterning strategy suitable for solution‑processable organic semiconductors, enabling high‑fidelity OECT arrays with suppressed leakage and off‑state currents, on/off ratios up to 107, and good device‑to‑device uniformity. Leveraging these patterned OECTs, we demonstrate the integration of logic circuits, including complementary inverters as well as NAND and NOR gates, exhibiting high voltage gain (up to 203 V/V), large noise margins (92.17%) and stable operation at low supply voltages. Benefiting from the patterning approach and the AlOx-induced suppression of off-state current, the static power consumption of the inverter is reduced by approximately 90%. Beyond digital logic, we further show that these devices emulate key synaptic functions such as short-term plasticity and enable reservoir computing tasks, defining a manufacturable platform for integrated bioelectronic circuitry and neuromorphic architectures. This patterning strategy therefore provides a general route toward high-density biointerfaces and next-generation brain-computer interfaces.

### 5. [Neuromorphic memory effect in aerosol-jet-printed thick-PEDOT:PSS organic electrochemical transistors](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/382eb0abb4612e6036964432495042099bcc346e)

**Authors:** C. Pérez-Wences, J. A. Ávila-Niño, E. Araujo-Palomo, Francisco Javier Ibarra-Villegas  
**Venue/category:** Physica Scripta  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.1088/1402-4896/aeb633`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 55  
**Matched:** organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, pedot:pss, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) based on poly(3,4ethylenedioxythiophene) doped with poly (styrene sulfonate) (PEDOT:PSS) semiconductor have emerged as promising candidates for neuromorphic devices due to their mixed ionic-electronic transport of this polymer and their ability to emulate biological synaptic behavior. In this work, we report on the fabrication and characterization of flexible thick-film PEDOT:PSS OECTs produced by Aerosol Jet Printing (AJP) to explore the relationships among drain bias and memory performance in thick PEDOT:PSS channels. We found that these devices exhibited both short-term and longterm memory dynamics, depending on the applied gate-pulse number and pulse duration. We found that thick PEDOT:PSS channels displayed enhanced hysteresis in transfer curves, consistent with a larger ionic reservoir that allows prolonged charge trapping and a slower dedoping. A systematic study of the drain voltage (V D ) revealed that higher |Vᴅ| values enhance the memory level by increasing both the current modulation and transconductance, as a larger fraction of the PEDOT:PSS channel participates in the electrochemical redox process, despite a memory window shows a non-monotonic behavior.Instead of relying exclusively on the conventional voltage memory window, an analysis of the effective memory window, which was defined as the enclosed area of the forward and backward transfer characteristics, was proposed to evaluate the effective ionic memory behavior of the OECTs, finding a linear trend with respect to V D . These findings highlight the effect of drain bias in thick PEDOT:PSS channels offering design guidelines for future bioinspired, and memory-based printed and flexible organic electronics.

### 6. [High-Performance Low Threshold Voltage n-Type OECTs via a Stepwise Cyanation of Quinoxaline-Based Polymeric Semiconductor.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/b1af716d7176cd8b56491efc72551cf29307e59a)

**Authors:** Song-Lin Mo, Guo-Hong Hu, Chao Deng, Y.-Z. Wan, Shao-Dan Cai, Hong-Xiang Li, Liang-Wen Feng, Wei Huang, Jian-Hua Chen  
**Venue/category:** Advances in Materials  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1002/adma.75397`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 52  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Achieving both low threshold voltage (Vth) and high performance in organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) is critical for low-power, high-sensitivity bioelectronics, yet simultaneously achieving low Vth and high device performance remains a long-standing challenge. Herein, we propose a stepwise cyanation strategy to modify quinoxaline-based polymeric semiconductors to mitigate this dilemma. Benefiting from the strong electron-withdrawing ability of cyano substituents, QxCN-CNTVT functionalized with cyano groups on both donor and acceptor units exhibits a deep LUMO level of -4.23 eV, one of the deepest among reported n-type ionic-electronic conductors. As a result, OECTs based on QxCN-CNTVT demonstrate excellent comprehensive performance, with a high µC* value of up to 168.89 F cm-1 V-1 s-1 and a low Vth of 0.13 V, while maintaining superior operational stability (retaining ∼80% of the initial current after 10,000 s of continuous operation and 10,000 full switching cycles). Furthermore, QxCN-CNTVT is successfully applied to complementary inverters for efficient amplification of electrooculogram (EOG) and electrocardiogram (ECG) signals, as well as NAND and NOR logic gates for integrated logic functions. This work not only alleviates the widely observed imbalance between performance and Vth in n-type OECTs but also provides a versatile molecular design strategy for next-generation low-power bioelectronic devices.

### 7. [Myelin Sheath-Inspired Fluorocarbon Hydrophobic Bridges for Stable n-Type Organic Mixed Ionic–Electronic Conductors](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/bfc23d030df412024dd709ebe15ed72c765dac7f)

**Authors:** Wang Feng, Shao-Dan Cai, Chao Deng, Bei Zhang, Meng-Ge Wu, Jian-Hua Chen, A. Facchetti  
**Venue/category:** Journal of the American Chemical Society  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1021/jacs.6c12196`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 50  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, omiec, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Operationally stable n-type organic mixed ionic–electronic conductors (OMIECs) remain a critical bottleneck for organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) for application in bioelectronic and neuromorphic devices. However, several state-of-the-art n-type OMIECs suffer from performance decay and poor stability when operated in aqueous media. Inspired by myelin sheaths, we challenge the conventional “π-backbone + hydrophilic side-chain” design and propose a molecular architecture with fluorocarbon hydrophobic bridges to spatially segregate, at the molecular level, ionic and electronic pathways. By tuning the fluorocarbon bridge chain length in fluorocarbon-ethylene glycol chain substituents [−CH2(CF2)nCH2(OCH2CH2)5OMe = 2nFG; n = 1, 2, 3, 4, 6] of a DPP-CNTVT polymer series (2nFG-P), we balance electron transport, ion exchange, redox chemistry efficiency, and water incorporation. Extensive in situ spectroelectrochemistry, EIS, Raman, EQCM-D, AFM, and GIWAXS characterization reveals that tuning the length of the hydrophobic bridge enables good electron transport, suppresses excessive water incorporation, and enhances stability. Thus, the polymer with a mid-length bridge, 8FG-P (n = 4), achieves the greatest electron transport (μC* ∼ 157 F cm–1 V–1 s–1, μ ∼ 1.22 cm2 V–1 s–1), fastest response (τon/τoff = 2.6/1.5 ms), and retains >90% of the initial performance after 10,000 s of operation in water, a result independent of the polymer molecular weight. This strategy potentially expands OMIECs design principles and offers useful insights toward stable n-type OECT semiconductors.

### 8. [Scalable Ion‐Electron Coupled Poly(Benzodifurandione) Inks Enabling Biosignal Amplification and Neuromorphic Memory](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/827b0a6a740bd08c09162b4e841bd80cf6af449a)

**Authors:** Ying-Jun Chen, M. Stephen, Qiang He, Hao-Nan Sun, Wei Lin Leong  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Materials & Technologies  
**Date:** 2026-10-10  
**DOI:** `10.1002/admt.71400`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 49  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface, organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Printed organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) are promising for next‐generation biosensing and neuromorphic computing, owing to their high transconductance, low operating voltage, mechanical flexibility, and efficient volumetric ionic–electronic coupling. Yet, printable n ‐type OECTs have lagged their p ‐type counterparts due to limited stability and poor printability. The key challenge lies in achieving high electronic conductivity, efficient ionic accessibility, and rheological properties compatible with scalable printing. To address this, we developed a screen‐printable, air‐stable, n ‐type OECT channel by blending high‐conductivity poly(benzodifurandione) (PBFDO) with poly(ethylene oxide) (PEO). Devices fabricated using 5wt.% PEO blend and a gelatin‐based electrolyte exhibited tunable transient responses (turn‐on time; 9.6 to 419 ms, turn‐off time; 4.3 ms to 17.8 ms), stable operation (92.4% ON current retained over 1,000 cycles), and high uniformity across 50 devices (I ds, ON = 2.47 ± 0.29 mA and transconductance g m = 3.55 ± 0.57 mS). PEO transforms low‐viscosity PBFDO solutions into shear‐thinning pastes suitable for screen printing, while enhancing film wettability and electrolyte infiltration. This dual role promotes efficient ion penetration and improves printability, enabling devices with improved transient speeds and rheological properties optimized for scalable fabrication. By controlling channel thickness, thin devices enable electrocardiogram signal acquisition while thick devices act as artificial synapses, highlighting PBFDO‐based inks as versatile platforms for integrated bioelectronic interfaces.

### 9. [Hydrogel-Gated Organic Electrochemical Transistors for Wearable and Portable Direct Detection of Aerosol-Borne Viral Antigens](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/c3bac673192d56aa0d21578a6786fafbbb53acce)

**Authors:** Hai-Yang Yu, Yu-Han Lu, Bi-Han Guo, Jia-Meng Wang, Ya Zhang, Bing-Fang Wang, Qiu-Yu Yang, Lan Shen, Mengtian Shan, Fei-Long Wang, Dai-Zong Ji, Yin Fang  
**Venue/category:** Gels  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.3390/gels12100916`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 48  
**Matched:** organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Direct detection of viral antigens in respiratory aerosols is challenging because affinity-based organic electrochemical transistors (OECTs) typically rely on liquid samples. Here, a non-flowing agarose hydrogel replaced the liquid electrolyte to capture aerosols, conduct ions, and support target transport. Before OECT readout, a separate 1-min ring-electrode field-assistance step was applied to facilitate target delivery to the aptamer-functionalized gate. Under the optimized conditions, the device achieved a solution-phase concentration-based limit of detection (LoD) of 1.27 fM and a sample-to-result time of 17 min. The approach was implemented in face-mask and handheld formats with a portable wireless readout system. In nebulization-based tests using SARS-CoV-2 spike protein, the LoDs expressed in terms of the concentrations of the nebulizer source solutions were 6.89 pM and 1.19 pM for the face-mask and handheld configurations, respectively. These results demonstrate that hydrogel-mediated aerosol capture and ionic coupling, ring-electrode field assistance, and OECT transduction can be integrated to enable direct electrical detection of aerosol-borne viral antigens, supporting further development of wearable and portable respiratory screening systems.

### 10. [Multichannel, Portable and Low-Cost Source Measure Unit Platform for the Continuous Characterization of Organic Electrochemical Transistor Biosensors](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/cb71a32bdb5258bc246360eb3f5fe780cf76e3eb)

**Authors:** M. Campanini, Lorenzo Vigna, D. Vurro, P. D'Angelo, Martina Cicolini, S. Marasso, M. Cocuzza, A. Ballesio  
**Venue/category:** Italian National Conference on Sensors  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.3390/s26206526`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 47  
**Matched:** organic electrochemical transistor, organic electrochemical transistors, oect, electrochemical transistor, biosensor  

**Abstract**

Organic Electrochemical Transistors (OECTs) have emerged as continuous monitoring sensors for in vitro models. Source Measure Units (SMUs) are typical instruments used to simultaneously control input and output signals in OECTs. Conventional SMUs are unsuitable for in vitro applications due to their limited channel count, large footprint and high cost. This work presents the design and development of a multichannel, portable and cost-effective SMU platform for the continuous and long-term characterization of OECT biosensors. The hardware is based on a breadboard interfacing via custom connections to a six-OECT setup integrated into a standard culture plate and operated within a cell incubator. System functionality is managed by an Arduino Nano microcontroller (Arduino, Monza, Italy) through custom-developed software, enabling automated characterization of the OECTs. Platform performance was evaluated by comparing OECT I–V characteristics and time responses against the commercial B2912A SMU (Keysight Technologies, Santa Rosa, CA, USA), using phosphate-buffered saline (PBS) to mimic biological samples. Long-term continuous operation was verified through a 5 day recording session with a 30 min sampling interval. Experimental results demonstrated consistent functionality with satisfactory accuracy for the targeted application. The proposed platform is a promising alternative to conventional SMUs for the continuous and long-term monitoring of OECT biosensors. Future work will focus on hardware optimization and full biological validation through OECT-based monitoring of in vitro models.

### 11. [Cognitive Materials Engineering for Bioelectricity and Bioelectronics: Research and Applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/86a7de35d7b74a31df4fbdf522ce821cfc38a22d)

**Authors:** Massimo Mariello, M. Djamgoz  
**Venue/category:** Bioelectricity  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.1177/25763113261496183`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 44  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface, neural interface, neural interfaces, biosensor  

**Abstract**

Cognitive materials engineering represents an emerging paradigm in which artificial intelligence (AI) is incorporated directly into the design, selection, and operation of bioelectrical and bioelectronic materials. Rather than relying on static material properties or fixed stimulation protocols, AI-enabled material systems can adapt their electrical, mechanical, and chemical characteristics in response to dynamic biological states. This perspective examines how machine learning, deep learning, reinforcement learning, and neuromorphic approaches can guide material chemistry, scaffold assembly, and real-time functional tuning in bioelectronic interfaces. Focusing on neural interfaces, cancer bioelectricity, wearable biosensors, and tissue engineering, we outline how adaptive materials and predictive models may enable next-generation bioelectronic platforms that learn from biological feedback and coevolve with living tissues.

### 12. [Biodegradable Capacitive-Coupling Bioelectronics for Wireless Electrical Stimulation and Longitudinal Electrophysiological Monitoring of Peripheral Nerve Regeneration.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/6d7f39d50861b4c7026002cd2085d78050ae4899)

**Authors:** Yun-Jie Jin, Ren-Yuan Sun, Wen-Liang Liu, Xian-Zhen Dong, Yi-Ru Yang, Shi-Cong Chen, Chun-Lin Wen, Kun Yang, Wei-Lin Xu, Chao Xu, Zhi-Qiang Luo, Hong-Jun Yang  
**Venue/category:** Advances in Materials  
**Date:** 2026-10-21  
**DOI:** `10.1002/adma.75437`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 43  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, implantable bioelectronics, electrophysiology, electrophysiological, peripheral nerve  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT Electrical stimulation accelerates peripheral nerve regeneration, yet current strategies are limited by wired interfaces, percutaneous leads, nondegradable components, and poor capacity for dynamic functional monitoring within the same subject. Here, we report biodegradable capacitive‐coupling bioelectronics (CCB), an implantable platform integrating wireless electrical stimulation, regenerative guidance, and longitudinal electrophysiological monitoring. The CCB comprises biodegradable molybdenum electrodes, Mo‐wire interconnects, and a textile‐reinforced electroactive bioelectronic nerve guidance conduit (Bio‐NGC). Capacitive coupling between external and implanted electrodes enables battery‐free wireless delivery of electrical cues to regenerating nerves, while the same architecture allows repeated recording of regenerated compound nerve action potentials (CNAPs). In a rat sciatic nerve defect model, CCB‐mediated stimulation enhances Schwann cell (SC) activation, mitochondrial bioenergetics, axonal regeneration, remyelination, angiogenesis, and motor recovery, with multiple outcomes approaching those of autograft repair. Mechanistic analyses revealed coordinated neurovascular remodeling, pro‐regenerative immune modulation, calcium‐related signaling, AMPK‐associated energy metabolism, and neurotrophic factor expression. Importantly, CCB‐enabled monitoring captured regeneration trajectories in the same animal without repeated nerve exposure, showing temporal trends consistent with conventional wired electrophysiology. This work establishes a biodegradable therapeutic‐monitoring bioelectronic platform and advances peripheral nerve repair from passive conductive scaffolds toward intelligent implantable bioelectronics.

### 13. [Two‐Dimensional Framework Materials as Building Blocks for Next‐Generation Intelligent Biointerfaces](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/59e92e3524ebc1c535155f4e9c4e2b6be8350fe6)

**Authors:** Hui-Hui Zhao, Xing-Wen Zhou, Hong-Yun Zhang, Hong-Wei Li, Hai-Bo Huang, Li-Guo Chen, Yong-Xiang Li, Xin-Yi Hu, D. Zhang, Li-Ning Sun, Guan-Yu Chen  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Functional Materials  
**Date:** 2026-10-11  
**DOI:** `10.1002/adfm.78531`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 41  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, wearable bioelectronics, biointerface, neural interface, neural interfaces  

**Abstract**

The convergence of neural interfaces, wearable bioelectronics, and human‐machine interaction is driving intelligent biointerfaces capable of sensing and responding to neural, physiological, and biomechanical signals. Realizing this vision demands a substantial advance in sensing and actuation hardware, which remains constrained by trade‐offs in conventional bulk materials. This review proposes that two‐dimensional framework materials, encompassing intrinsic 2D crystals, heterostructures, composites, and low‐dimensional architectures, provide a transformative platform to overcome these barriers. The application scope is organized into five categories: implantable neural interfaces, non‐invasive neural interfaces, physiological sensing, wearable human‐machine interfaces, and soft actuation or haptic feedback. We establish a three‐tier architecture. First, we catalog the requirements of piezoresistive, piezoelectric, capacitive, electromagnetic, electrostatic, and electrothermal sensors and actuators, identifying performance bottlenecks in bulk materials. Second, we trace these ceilings to microscopic origins: continuous electronic density of states, strong dielectric screening, coupled elastic‐thermal phonon transport, and weakly tunable electron‐phonon coupling that lock sensitivity, compliance, energy efficiency, and thermal safety into trade‐offs. Third, we show how quantum confinement, attenuated screening, and broken symmetry in 2D materials decouple these constraints. We conclude by outlining a co‐design strategy integrating heavy‐element doping, symmetry engineering, interfacial design, and coupling phenomena to tailor 2D materials for next‐generation intelligent biointerfaces.

### 16. [Zwitterionic Fluorinated Phosphocholine Block Copolymer Nanoparticles with Aggregation-Induced Emission for Dual-Modality 19F MRI/Fluorescence Bioimaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/7dc495fab92a31ef44b631b93c3348a9fe18e176)

**Authors:** Wen-Ze Xiao, De-Shuo Wang, Juan Yu, De-Kun Huang, Su-Jing Liu, Jing-Jiang Sun, Jian-Jiang He, Qing-Fu Wang, Ya-Nan Li, Wei Zhao  
**Venue/category:** Biomacromolecules  
**Date:** 2026-10-20  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.biomac.6c02043`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 37  
**Matched:** bioimaging, fluorescence bioimaging, fluorescence imaging, optical imaging  

**Abstract**

Abstract Fluorine-19 magnetic resonance imaging (19F MRI) offers low-background, concentration-responsive imaging but suffers from low sensitivity and short transverse relaxation times. Aggregation-induced emission (AIE) fluorophores provide complementary optical imaging through strong fluorescence upon aggregation. Here, fluorinated amphiphilic block copolymers were prepared by reversible addition−fragmentation chain-transfer (RAFT) polymerization of dimethylaminoethyl methacrylate (DMAEMA) and tetraphenylethylene acrylate (TPEA), followed by phosphocholine fluorination. The resulting PTPE-PFCP nanoparticles self-assembled into core−shell structures with a hydrophobic AIE-active PTPE core and a hydrophilic fluorinated phosphocholine corona. The optimized nanoparticles showed well-resolved 19F signals, with T1 and T2 values of 0.222 and 0.184 s, respectively, and gave readily detectable 19F MRI signals. Fluorescence increased over 6 h, while cell viability remained above 70% at 100 μM, with time-dependent cellular fluorescence consistent with nanoparticle uptake. This work presents a feasible strategy for fluorinated AIE-active copolymers as dual-mode 19F MRI/fluorescence imaging probes.

### 18. [Development and Innovative Applications of Tools for Monitoring Neuronal Electrical Activity](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/33645809190e3614e3766e210d29bca277b305c1)

**Authors:** Yue-Ya Mu  
**Venue/category:** Theoretical and Natural Science  
**Date:** 2026-10-20  
**DOI:** `10.54254/2753-8818/2026.es38045`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 36  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, neural interface, neural interfaces, flexible electrode, brain-computer interface  

**Abstract**

Neuronal electrical activity provides the most direct dynamic readout of excitability, synaptic transmission, circuit coordination, and information processing in the nervous system. Monitoring tools have evolved from intracellular and patch-clamp recordings that resolve ion-channel and membrane currents to high-density extracellular arrays, flexible neural interfaces, optical activity indicators, wireless implants, and artificial intelligence-assisted decoding systems. Each modality captures a different level of neural organization. Patch-clamp recording provides high biophysical precision at the single-cell scale, microelectrode arrays and silicon probes enable population recordings across networks, electroencephalography and functional magnetic resonance imaging support noninvasive human studies, and optical sensors map calcium, voltage, or neurochemical dynamics with cellular or subcellular resolution. Recent advances in ultraflexible electrodes, mesh electronics, genetically encoded indicators, nanomaterials, closed-loop bioelectronics, and machine learning have shifted neural monitoring from episodic measurement toward chronic, multimodal, and intervention-linked systems. This review summarizes traditional and emerging tools for monitoring neuronal electrical activity, analyzes multimodal integration strategies, and discusses applications in disease mechanism research, brain-computer interfaces, neuromodulation, brain-inspired intelligence, and neural engineering. Translation will depend on long-term biocompatibility, stable signal quality, interpretable computational models, and standards that connect device performance with biological validity.

### 19. [Autonomous Reconfigurable Modular Conductive Hydrogels for Adaptive and Degradable Bioelectronic Interfaces](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/f097b0656d572f61e439159b4c2ed8fcca7117ae)

**Authors:** Matthew K. Burgess, Zofia B. Wolny, Ze-Kun Liu, Karolina Cysewska, Malavika Nair  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Materials & Technologies  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.1002/admt.71406`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 35  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface, conductive hydrogel  

**Abstract**

Bioelectronic interfaces are increasingly used in medical devices for tissue stimulation and sensing. Transient bioelectronics seek the same functionality in temporary systems, yet most available materials are nondegradable, stiff, and require surgery or external removal. Achieving tissue‐like softness, smooth injectability, stable mixed conductivity, and reliable short‐term bioresorption within a single biocompatible matrix remains challenging. Here, we decouple injectability from bulk conductive performance using a modular reconfiguration strategy. High‐filler‐content hydrogels are converted into lyophilized modules that can be resuspended, injected through a standard syringe, and then autonomously reassemble in situ into a cohesive conductive network, recovering 90% of conductivity after freeze–drying, fragmentation, and reconfiguration. The platform uses a fully degradable tannic‐acid‐crosslinked poly(vinyl alcohol) matrix with molybdenum and molybdenum trioxide nanoparticles as co‐functional fillers. Mixed ionic/electronic conduction arises from redox‐active tannic acid–Mo(VI) complexes (ionic transport) and semi‐percolated Mo nanoparticles (electron hopping). The optimized formulation matches extracellular‐matrix mechanics (Young's modulus 0.03 MPa; failure strain 998%) and fully degrades within 120 h under simulated physiological conditions. In a human skin explant wound model, conformal integration enables charge transfer, reaching 0.02 A at across a 1 cm defect.

### 20. [Prepressed Liquid Metal Fabric with Strain-Insensitive Conductance for Wearable Bioelectronics](https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.6c15065)

**Authors:** Mengying Luo, Jing Tang, Jiale Zhao, Ke Chen, Leran Liu, Weibing Zhong, Kangqi Chang, Kangyu Jia, Mufang Li, Dong Wang  
**Venue/category:** ACS Applied Materials & Interfaces  
**Date:** 2026-10-23  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acsami.6c15065`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 34  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, wearable bioelectronics, pedot:pss  

**Abstract**

Abstract The development of flexible conductors with high electrical conductivity, flexibility, and electromechanical stability is important for reliable signal transmission in wearable electronics. Here, we report a patternable liquid metal (LM) fabric with strain-insensitive performance, fabricated using a highly elastic knitted fabric (KF) as the substrate and a gallium-based liquid metal as the conductive coating. A patterned poly(3,4-ethylenedioxythiophene)(styrene sulfonate) (PEDOT:PSS) intermediate layer was introduced to enhance the interfacial adhesion and facilitate the selective deposition of LM on the textile substrate. In addition, pre‑oxidation of the LM followed by a prepressing treatment further promotes the permeation of liquid metal into the knitted fabric, thereby improving the stability of electrical conductivity. Benefiting from the pore structure of the fabric and the strong interfacial combination of liquid metal and PEDOT:PSS, this composite fabric achieves a strain capacity of 200% with low resistance variations, and the resistance change rates are 18.6% for longitudinal stretching, 20.4% for 45° diagonal stretching, and 24.8% for transverse stretching. When stretched by 100% along three distinct directions (transverse, longitudinal, and 45°), the resistance change rate remains below 5% in all cases, demonstrating excellent multidirectional strain-insensitive conductivity. Moreover, after 1000 cyclic stretching cycles under a 30% strain, the resistance fluctuation still stays within 3%. The resulting fabric exhibited excellent conductivity stability when applied to conductors, Joule heating, electrocardiogram (ECG), and near field communication (NFC), demonstrating its potential for next‑generation wearable electronics.

### 21. [Advances in Biodegradable Supercapacitors for Implantable Bioelectronics: Materials, Energy Storage, Degradation, and Clinical Translation.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/322134dc4811ddef20e0dcff7340225dcd66b329)

**Authors:** Kundali Yadav, Dhilip Kumar Chinnalagu, Jaya Kumar Ponraj, Aniruddha Kundu, Vinodkumar Etacheri, S. Sahoo  
**Venue/category:** Small  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1002/smll.76270`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 34  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, implantable bioelectronics, biosensor  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT The emergence of transient bioelectronics has accelerated the development of biodegradable supercapacitors as promising energy‐storage systems for temporary medical implants, including neural stimulators, cardiac pacemakers, controlled drug delivery devices, and biosensors. Biodegradable supercapacitors can safely resorb after completing their function, eliminating secondary surgical removal and reducing long‐term foreign‐body complications, unlike conventional batteries. This review comprehensively discusses recent advances in degradable supercapacitors, focusing on biocompatible polymers, bioresorbable electrodes, separators, electrolytes, and current collectors. This review critically evaluates the electrochemical performance of biodegradable supercapacitors under physiological conditions, including capacitance retention, cycling stability, and degradation behaviour. Current challenges, such as limited energy density, mechanical instability, precise bioresorption kinetics, and scalability, are discussed. This study addresses key regulatory and safety considerations, including biocompatibility, toxicity, immunogenicity, and pathway approval. Further, outlines strategic future directions: bio‐derived carbon, biologically synthesized conductors, self‐healing hydrogel electrolytes, stimulus‐responsive programmable degradation, and energy‐harvesting integration. Emerging frontiers at the intersection of bioelectronics, neuromorphic computing, and living devices, and advocates for standardized benchmarking and circular‐economy integration. This comprehensive analysis aims to accelerate the rational design, validation, and clinical translation of degradable supercapacitors toward autonomous, patient‐friendly implantable systems.

### 23. [Paintable In Situ Gelling Bioelectronic Interfaces for Epidermal Electrophysiological Monitoring.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/dc2b79e0cdf38dbc313db4047a97542260187c5b)

**Authors:** Long-Chao Huang, Xue-Lan Li, Yun-Shu Yang, Yue Sun, Sheng-Long Wang, Bo-Ling Lan, Guo Tian, Tao Yang, Tian-Pei Xu, Yong Ao, Zhi-Yuan Gao, Jin-Long Sun et al.  
**Venue/category:** ACS Applied Materials and Interfaces  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acsami.6c18381`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 32  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface, electrophysiological, e-skin  

**Abstract**

Noninvasive epidermal electrophysiological monitoring is frequently constrained by unstable electrode-skin contact interfaces over soft, textured, and dynamically deforming skin. The predefined geometries of conventional electrodes limit their capacity to conform to local microtopography. Here, we introduce an in situ gelation strategy performed directly on the epidermis to construct a conformal ionic bioelectronic interface. A fluidic precursor comprising gelatin and tannic acid is initially stabilized against premature gelation via competitive solvent-solute interactions. Upon application, solvent evaporation and gelatin reassembly induce rapid network formation. This process directly yields a highly conformal and gently detachable interface with remarkably low contact impedance. The resulting hydrated dynamic network provides mechanical compliance, robust interfacial adhesion, stretch-tolerant ionic conduction, and cooling-induced interfacial weakening that enables gentle removal. The on-skin-gelled interface reduces skin-electrode contact impedance to 30 kΩ at 10 Hz, compared with 191 kΩ for commercial conductive pastes, and increases the signal-to-noise ratio from 24.9 to 27.8 dB. In proof-of-concept demonstrations, the interface supports within-subject electromyography-based gesture recognition with a classification accuracy of 93.4% and electroencephalography recordings of eye-state-dependent α-rhythm modulation as well as short-term sleep-related waveforms. These findings underscore the potential of on-skin in situ gelation as a versatile approach to fabricating paintable ionic bioelectronic interfaces for advanced electrophysiological monitoring.

### 27. [Boosting NIR-II Luminescence via Ca2+-Formed Band Structure Modulation in Lanthanide-Doped Nanoparticles for Deep-Tissue Bioimaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/75cf17b62666d2904de003476057deb4ea830e62)

**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.nanolett.6c02932`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 30  
**Matched:** bioimaging, biological imaging, nir-ii  

**Abstract**

Abstract Lanthanide-doped nanoparticles with near-infrared-II (NIR-II, 1000–1700 nm) emission hold great promise for deep-tissue bioimaging, yet their luminescence efficiency is often limited by conventional sensitization pathways. Herein, we go beyond this conventional paradigm by introducing a Ca2+-formed band structure modulation in the host lattice to dramatically boost the NIR-II emission of lanthanide-doped nanoparticles. Rational doping of Ca2+ ions restructures the energy levels, enabling the probe to efficiently capture excitation energy and channel it toward NIR-II-emitting lanthanide activators. This Ca2+-induced band structure modulation mechanism not only enhances the absorption cross section but also accelerates energy transfer to the NIR-II-emitting states. As a result, the designed nanoprobes exhibit excellent photostability and a high quantum yield. In vivo bioimaging demonstrates that the resulting nanoprobes enable deep-tissue visualization with an improved signal-to-background ratio. This work establishes a sensitization strategy that goes beyond conventional approaches, positioning lanthanide-based NIR-II probes as promising candidates for advanced biological imaging.

### 28. [Impact of Backbone Fluorination on Self-Assembly and Mixed Conduction of Isoindigo-Based Polymers](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/d10ac4f43dd7a2029c7d6adadcb3ab131d0ccf7d)

**Authors:** Rebecca F. Meacham, Camille E. Cunin, Geon Gug Yang, Saroj Upreti, Wen-Hao Li, Sanket Samal, Robert Posey, Joshua Tropp, Yan Zhao, Xiao-Dan Gu, Aristide Gumyusenge  
**Venue/category:** Macromolecules  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.macromol.6c01586`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 30  
**Matched:** organic electrochemical transistor, oect, conducting polymer, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

Fluorination is a common method to induce backbone planarity in semiconducting polymers via noncovalent interactions that effectively lock the rotation of single bonds along the backbone. Often explored in dry-state devices, fluorination of isoindigo polymer backbones in mixed conductors remains relatively unexplored. Herein, we investigated systematic fluorination of a family of isoindigo-based mixed conducting polymers with hybrid ethylene glycol-based side chains to probe the impact of fluorination on the resulting assembly and electrochemical performance. Through UV–Vis–NIR, FTIR, Raman spectroscopy, and electrochemical methods, we showed that fluorination effectively planarizes portions of the backbone depending on the fluorination location, even with ethylene glycol side chains, and the placement of fluorine atoms along the backbone is crucial toward organic electrochemical transistor (OECT) device performance. Electronic conduction was found to be improved upon fluorinating the isoindigo acceptor unit in OECTs, but it came at the cost of decreased volumetric ionic capacitance. Further, fluorinating the bithiophene donor inhibited p-type conduction in OECTs. As a result, systematic fluorination revealed the importance of understanding the nuanced design requirements of isoindigo-based mixed conductors for the optimal balance of both modes of conduction in aqueous environments.

### 29. [Different modalities of time-resolved two-photon fluorescence lifetime measurements for energy transfer analysis of donor-linker-acceptor construct](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/58ffe17b3c2e74d1af4aac711f8a6cf652a00392)

**Authors:** Gabriela Núñez-Gotia, Isaac Hanauska, Rylee Rosenberg, Malachy Brink, E. Olson, Harper Wittmers, E. Sheets, Ahmed A. Heikal  
**Venue/category:** Ultrafast Nonlinear Imaging and Spectroscopy XIV  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1117/12.3114472`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 30  
**Matched:** biosensor, two-photon fluorescence, fluorescence lifetime imaging, flim  

**Abstract**

Förster resonance energy transfer (FRET) is a powerful tool for in vivo and in vitro studies of protein-protein interactions and conformational changes of complex biological molecules. For FRET studies in live cells and tissues, fluorescence lifetime imaging microscopy (FLIM) is advantageous due to the combined spatio-temporal resolution that provides a morphological context for cellular protein-protein interactions. However, these FLIM measurements suffer from some technical and experimental-design compromises to maximize the fluorescence detection efficiency and reduce the exposure time of living cells to pulsed laser (i.e., minimize photodamage) during measurements. For example, the magic-angle fluorescence polarization detection that is essential to rule out the effect of rotational dynamics during the excited-state lifetime is usually ruled out in FLIM experimental design. In addition, the computer-generated instrument response function (IRF) is routinely used for deconvolution data analysis in FLIM measurements for convenience instead of a measured IRF under the same experimental conditions. Here we investigate the FRET efficiency of an ionic-strength biosensor, namely, mCerulean3-linker-mCitrine (or RD), as a model system using different modalities of time-resolved 2P-fluorescence measurements as a function of potassium chloride concentration. We also examined the effects of different aspects of experimental designs (magic-angle polarization detection) and data analysis (IRF, FRET efficiency models) of 2P-FLIM approach as compared with the single-point time-resolved 2P-fluorescence measurements as a benchmark technique. These comparative studies are beneficial to researchers who are using quantitative and non-invasive FLIM for investigating of protein-protein interactions and conformational changes in living cells.

### 30. [Bridging Biosensing and Hardware-Level Data Security With a Conjugated Radical Polymer.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/7acba1e526794255d14cd9b40863ccd7cf514bda)

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 29  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, biointegrated, organic electrochemical transistor, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

The integration of biological signal processing with hardware-level data security remains a fundamental challenge in bioelectronic systems, where sensing and data protection are typically implemented as separate functional layers. Here, we present a conjugated radical polymer platform that exhibits stochastic electronic behavior and bioelectronic signal transduction within a single material system. The system displays stochastic resistive switching associated with its complex electronic structure and structural disorder, which enables physical unclonable function characteristics with uniqueness and uniformity approaching the ideal value of 50%. In parallel, the same material exhibits electrochemical signal transduction in an organic electrochemical transistor configuration, allowing highly sensitive dopamine detection with a detection limit of ∼10 pM, and a preferential response over the tested interferents. Systematic analysis indicates that the sensing response is consistent with redox-mediated coupling between dopamine and the radical-containing backbone. By establishing a material-level strategy that links stochastic electronic functionality and bioelectronic sensing, this work provides a pathway toward secure, adaptive, and biointegrated electronic systems.

### 33. [Multiplexed intravascular molecular imaging using dual-channel near-infrared fluorescence integrated with optical coherence tomography](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/f3c7e20c30613b64fb14bf7dc2df585aa69a5084)

**Authors:** Yeon Hoon Kim, D. Kang, Yong Geun Lim, Hyeong Soo Nam, Hyun Jung Kim, Ryeong Hyun Kim, J. Kim, J. Song, Kyeongsoon Park, J. W. Kim, Hongki Yoo

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 28  
**Matched:** fluorescence imaging, molecular imaging, near-infrared fluorescence, in vivo imaging  

**Abstract**

Accurate characterization of high-risk atherosclerotic plaques requires both structural and molecular information. While integrated optical coherence tomography (OCT)/near-infrared fluorescence (NIRF) imaging enables simultaneous assessment of plaque morphology and biological activity, single-target molecular detection may be insufficient to characterize the multifactorial plaque progression. Multiplexed intravascular molecular imaging remains technically challenging due to fluorescence crosstalk between emission channels, which compromises signal specificity. Here, we present a temporally modulated dual-channel NIRF/OCT catheter system designed to suppress spectral crosstalk and enable simultaneous multiplexed molecular imaging with structural information within the vessel wall. Time-interleaved excitation synchronized to the OCT trigger generates two NIRF excitation signals with a half-cycle phase difference, enabling crosstalk-free detection of two distinct molecular targets, as verified by phantom experiments. In vivo imaging in a rabbit model of atherosclerosis, using probes targeting calcified regions associated with osteogenic activity (ALN-PEG-Cy5.5) and mannose receptor (MMR)-expressing cells, including specific macrophage subset (MMR-PEG-Cy7), enabled simultaneous visualization of distinct molecular signatures within OCT-defined plaque structures. Ex vivo fluorescence imaging and immunohistochemistry supported the biological relevance of each NIRF channel. This integrated multimodal catheter system advances intravascular imaging from single-marker detection toward multiplexed molecular assessment of plaques, which could enable a more comprehensive evaluation of plaque pathobiology.

### 34. [Plant Crude Polysaccharide‐Enabled Hydrogel Electrodes for Long‐Term Stable EMG Recording](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/411f9829bb1d62b8d0c5dbf1dd114f85221d7b54)

**Abstract**

Conductive hydrogels with high conductivity, flexibility, skin compatibility, and long‐term stability are essential for reliable bioelectrical signal recording in flexible bioelectronic interfaces for wearable health monitoring and human‐machine interaction. However, additive‐based enhancement often disrupts the intrinsic hydrogel network, leading to trade‐offs among conductivity, flexibility, biocompatibility, and long‐term stability. To address this challenge, we propose a conductive hydrogel formation strategy based on the endogenous synergy of plant‐derived polysaccharides. As a representative system, we constructed a gelatin/PCA‐Na hydrogel incorporating safflower crude polysaccharides, which was denoted as GPP. Plant‐derived polysaccharide systems contain abundant hydrophilic groups and active components, including phenolic and ketone groups, which introduce multiple hydrogen bonds and non‐covalent interactions into the hydrogel network. As a result, GPP exhibits strong overall performance, with an adhesion strength of 50.1 kPa and a conductivity of 6.3 S m −1 . Moreover, it enables repeated EMG recordings during 7‐day continuous wear, with the signal‐to‐noise ratio remaining above 30 dB, indicating stable skin contact and reliable signal output. In addition, the endogenous synergy mechanism was extended to Prunella vulgaris crude polysaccharides. Overall, this work provides a plant‐derived endogenous synergy route toward safe, comfortable, and long‐term wearable bioelectronic interfaces.

### 36. [Nanomesh Bioelectronics Inspired by Basement Membrane for In Situ Monitoring of Oxidative Stress at the Alveolar-Capillary Barrier](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/f2d887da9e014652664363b1d7e20769b91fa0c2)

**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface  

**Abstract**

Abstract Inhalable pollutants can cross the alveolar-capillary barrier into the bloodstream, and pollutant-elicited oxidative stress at this barrier is a well-recognized pathogenic driver of systemic diseases. However, direct quantification of oxidative stress at the dynamic alveolar-capillary barrier has remained unattainable due to the lack of sensing tools, substantially limiting our understanding of pollutant toxicology and associated pathogenesis. Herein, we develop nanomesh bioelectronics that simultaneously function as a basement membrane analogue and an electrochemical sensor. The bioelectronic interface recapitulates the native basement membrane’s nanofibrous architecture, ultrathin geometry, high permeability, and mechanical properties, while exhibiting stable electrochemical responses to H2O2 under cyclic stretching. When incorporated into lung-on-a-chip, the bioelectronics enable simultaneous reconstruction of the alveolar-capillary barrier and in situ quantification of oxidative stress during respiratory stretching. Using benzo[a]pyrene as a model carcinogenic pollutant, we uncover a biphasic regulatory role of vascular endothelium: an intact endothelium reinforces epithelial resistance to pollutant stimuli, whereas endothelial dysfunction converts protective crosstalk into pathological oxidative amplification. Rat models further confirm that systemic vascular health shapes pulmonary responses to environmental insults. The proposed bioelectronics provide a versatile tool for engineering biological barriers and dissecting intercompartmental crosstalk.

### 37. [A Review on MXene and MXene-Based Composites for Advanced Biosensing and Bioimaging: Progress, Challenges, and Future Perspectives.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/1d1f338c10713ffc1fc08883decfb7d0845dc194)

**Abstract**

Abstract Recently, the growth of two-dimensional (2D) materials has opened innovative paths in nanobiotechnology, and MXenes have emerged as leading candidates owing to their superior physicochemical performance and functional adaptability. MXenes, a family of transition metal carbides, nitrides, and carbonitrides, reveal excellent electrical conductivity, large surface area, tuneable surface chemistry, hydrophilicity, and exceptional biocompatibility. These unique features make them highly attractive for biosensing and bioimaging applications. This review systematically abridges the recent advancements in MXene and its composites for biosensor development, containing electrochemical, optical, wearable, and flexible biosensors for the revealing of biomolecules, pathogens, cancer biomarkers, and glucose. The role of surface functionalization, hybridization with polymers, metal nanoparticles, carbon-based nanomaterials, and biomolecules in enhancing sensitivity, selectivity, and stability is critically deliberated. Moreover, the application of MXene composites in bioimaging approaches such as fluorescence imaging, photoacoustic imaging, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), and multimodal imaging is explored, emphasizing their photothermal conversion efficiency and contrast development competences. Various challenges associated with long-term stability, oxidation resistance, toxicity, and large-scale production were also addressed, along with future perspectives for clinical translation and next-generation smart biomedical platforms. Overall, MXene-based materials clamp significant potential to transform biosensing and bioimaging techniques via multifunctional and integrated diagnostic systems.

The combination of stimulated emission depletion (STED) microscopy with fluorescence lifetime imaging microscopy (FLIM) offers a powerful route to functional super-resolution imaging, but remains technically challenging for lifetime-based sensing because of the complex interplay between stimulated emission, photon budget, and accurate lifetime quantification. In this technical note, we evaluate and optimize STED-FLIM for nanoscale mapping of bacterial membrane mechanics using the mechanosensitive probe Flipper-TR. This probe is particularly demanding for STED-FLIM because it exhibits a biexponential fluorescence decay, with only the long lifetime reporting on membrane mechanical properties. Using liveBacillus subtiliscells, we establish an acquisition and analysis workflow that combines optimized imaging conditions with tail fitting to minimize distortions introduced by the depletion pulse. Under these conditions, STED imaging achieved a more than twofold improvement in spatial resolution compared with confocal microscopy, while maintaining sufficient photon counts for robust lifetime estimation. Finally, we discuss the trade-offs between spatial resolution and photon budget and provide practical recommendations for implementing STED-FLIM with environmentally sensitive probes. These results extend the application of Flipper-TR to functional super-resolution microscopy and provide a framework for robust STED-FLIM measurements of membrane nanomechanics and other fluorescence lifetime-based sensing applications.

### 41. [MRI-compatible 3D Soft Carbon Neural Interface Enabled by Versatile Depth-tunable Laser Drawing](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/98963f53efc00fbc6b4df2ed03c5bd2e5edb801c)

**Abstract**

Soft neural interfaces are designed to minimize mechanical mismatch with the brain. However, planar and recessed electrode sites remain locally decoupled from the curved and pulsating cortical surface, degrading the fidelity of neural sensing. Here, we report a 3D soft carbon electrode array, enabled by a depth-tunable laser drawing strategy. This laser-driven workflow achieves versatile fabrication of customizable 3D soft carbon neural interfaces, eliminating the reliance on conventional thin-film microfabrication processes. The fully silicone-based electrode structure realize device-scale conformality, and the 3D soft carbon sites promote a non-penetrating, intimate neural-electrode interface. The CNT-3D array combined superior in-vitro electrochemical and in-vivo recording performance compared with Au-2D and CNT-2D controls. Furthermore, in bilateral rabbit cortex implantation, CNT-3D array retained 80.4% of electrocorticography power after 4 months, versus 11.7% for CNT-2D control. Moreover, the 3D sensing sites significantly suppressed line-frequency harmonics and background noise interference. Subsequently, during magnetic resonance imaging, the CNT-3D array exhibits negligible implant-induced artifacts compared to the conventional Pt-Ir metal array. Overall, this work establishes a laser drawing strategy that extends neural interfaces from macro device-scale conformality to micro site-scale coupling, providing a technological pathway for practical and translational soft neurotechnology.

### 42. [Hydrogel Microneedle-Enabled In Situ Monitoring of Leaf Nitric Oxide for Evaluating NO Nanoprodrug-Mediated Salt Stress Alleviation in Maize.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/3bed32ee366aad4f6a34250d19e2e4d638098fb3)

**Authors:** Yu-Ying Wang, Jiayao Wulan, Qi-Liang Huang, Peng-Yue Zhao, Chong Cao, Yu-Qing Lin, Li-Dong Cao  
**Venue/category:** ACS Applied Materials and Interfaces

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 26  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface, conductive hydrogel  

**Abstract**

Chemical signals in living plants are spatially confined, rapidly consumed, and strongly dependent on the delivery history of exogenous regulators, creating a need for bioelectronic interfaces that can interrogate molecular dynamics directly within intact tissues. Here, we integrate a nitric oxide (NO) nanoprodrug with a conductive hydrogel microneedle platform to connect controlled molecular delivery, in planta electrochemical readout, and downstream biological responses in living maize. The working electrode comprises a poly(vinyl alcohol)/aramid nanofiber (PVA/ANF) hydrogel containing FeMo-N/C and an MXene conductive network. The hydrated microneedle architecture provides tissue-conformal contact and analyte transport, FeMo-N/C enhances the NO-associated electrochemical response, and MXene improves interfacial charge transfer. The resulting platform responds to NO from 1 nM to 100 μM with a detection limit of 0.3 nM and discriminates NO from representative electroactive plant interferents. Chitosan nanoparticles containing S-nitroso-mercaptosuccinic acid were used to moderate NO release, and the microneedle interface resolved treatment-dependent leaf signals over 7 days under salt stress. Signal attenuation by the NO scavenger cPTIO supported the NO-related origin of the electrochemical response. The time-resolved readout was accompanied by improved growth, reduced lipid peroxidation, changes in osmotic and antioxidant responses, and altered expression of stress-responsive genes. This delivery-bioelectronics-analysis framework provides a materials-based strategy for evaluating reactive nanoprodrugs and interrogating transient chemical signaling in living plants.

### 43. [Thickness‐Gradient Liquid Metal Connectors for Seamless Electrophysiological Sensors](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/3687f2baee549228929f1684f34bfff86f956125)

**Authors:** Jinyoung Kim, Jaejun Lee, Christopher E. Tabor, V. Tsukruk  
**Venue/category:** Advancement of science  
**Date:** 2026-10-16  
**DOI:** `10.1002/advs.77844`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 26  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic, wearable bioelectronics, electrophysiological  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT High‐fidelity electrophysiological monitoring requires wearable bioelectronic devices with mechanical properties closely matched to human skin. However, current approaches focus primarily on electrode design and performance, while overlooking mechanical compliance and interfacial continuity with complex and dynamic human skin. Mechanical mismatches across electrodes, connectors, and interconnects often lead to interfacial noise, motion artifacts, and electrical instability. In order to overcome these critical issues, we introduce a thickness‐gradient liquid metal (LM)/SEBS connector that enables seamless interfacial integration with skin, electrode, and interconnector interfaces through gradual mechanical compliance matching and manually activated metal network formation. The activated LM forms continuous and robust conductive networks, maintaining electrical stability under large tensile strain (∼1100%) and for repeatable deformation cycles. Skin‐mimetic conformal integration suggested here suppresses interfacial void formation and stress concentration and thereby preserves stable electrical pathways under dynamic skin deformation. As a result, the proposed system delivers markedly improved signal amplitude and signal‐to‐noise ratio, along with substantially lower noise, relative to conventional connector platforms. By systematically aligning mechanical compliance across sensor components, this mechanically adaptive design preserves seamless electrical pathways and minimizes motion‐induced artifacts under dynamic conditions, providing a robust platform for reliable electrophysiological monitoring in long‐lasting wearable bioelectronics.

### 44. [A Reconfigurable CMOS Microelectrode Array Featuring 24,576 Electrodes for Chemical and Electrical Neural Recording.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/a320a11e92de63856ea18aeb9dc778940309f48a)

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 26  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, neural interface, neural recording, electrophysiological  

**Abstract**

OBJECTIVE This study aims to develop a high throughput, high-density, and reconfigurable CMOS microelectrode array (MEA) chip for chemical and electrical recording of biological neural networks. METHODS The continuous time/discrete-time current sensing architecture is integrated on a single chip to support electrochemical and electrophysiological measurements within the same system. The readout achieves 24,576 electrodes, an input-referred noise density of 2 fA/√Hz, a bandwidth of 5 kHz/70 kHz, and 24/384 simultaneous output channels for chemical/electrical recording, respectively. For electrochemical measurements, inter-electrode chemical crosstalk is suppressed to improve the spatial resolution of array-based electrochemical measurements. RESULTS The proposed chip demonstrates electrochemical sensing at individual electrodes, quantitative detection of dopamine in prepared solution, and dynamic electrochemical imaging with high spatial and temporal resolution. For electrophysiological measurements, spontaneous extracellular biocurrent is successfully recorded from primary neurons confirming the capability of the system for cell-level electrophysiological current sensing. CONCLUSION Experimental results verify the applicability of the proposed MEA in recording electrochemical and electrophysiological signals from various biological samples, highlighting its potential as a versatile bioelectronic platform for multimodal neural interface applications. SIGNIFICANCE Compared with other MEAs, the proposed chip supports both neurotransmitter-related chemical sensing and neural current recording on the same hardware platform. Therefore, it provides a promising tool for studying the interaction between chemical and electrical signaling in biological neural networks.

### 45. [Topological Engineering of NIR-II Emissive Conjugated Oligomers With Unexpected Excited-State Manifolds for Efficient Phototheranostics and Cancer Immunotherapy.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/1828ce6b5f6fe5cd85a10ddacbe16ca7f6608bfd)

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 26  
**Matched:** fluorescence imaging, nir-ii, second near-infrared  

**Abstract**

Phototheranostic agents operating in the second near-infrared (NIR-II) window hold great potential for precision cancer therapy, yet the rational regulation of excited-state processes to simultaneously optimize fluorescence and phototherapeutic performances remains a fundamental challenge. Herein, we report a topology-driven excited-state engineering strategy for constructing conjugated oligomers with tailored excited-state manifolds and multi-channel excitation dynamics. By precisely manipulating molecular topology, conjugated topological oligomers achieve enhanced light-harvesting capability and optimized exciton utilization, thereby enabling synergistic regulation of excitation dynamics. Under 808 nm laser irradiation, nanoparticles (NPs) derived from TBT-3 exhibit 2.8-fold enhanced NIR-II fluorescence brightness, 2.7-fold improved photothermal performance, and 7.5-fold increased total reactive oxygen species (ROS) generation compared with TBT-1. In vivo studies demonstrate that TBT-3 NPs enable high-resolution NIR-II fluorescence imaging and efficient tumor elimination. Moreover, TBT-3-mediated phototherapy induces immunogenic cell death and enhances antitumor immunity, leading to improved therapeutic efficacy when combined with anti-PD-1 immune checkpoint blockade. This work provides a molecular topology-based strategy for manipulating excited-state landscapes and establishes conjugated oligomers as versatile candidates for NIR-II phototheranostics and cancer immunotherapy.

### 51. [A Versatile Aza-BODIPY-Based Activatable Probe for High-Contrast NIR-II Biosensing](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/aea27a4aa463d37eebee0710358757fc37c025da)

**Authors:** Chen-Zhi Yao, Li-Wen Huang, Qian He, Jia-Hao Guo, Shi-Zhong Luo, Zheng-Chun Yin, Xing-Cai Zhang, Feng Gao, Yong Fan, Ye Liu  
**Venue/category:** Analytical Chemistry  
**Date:** 2026-10-10  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.analchem.6c04759`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 24  
**Matched:** optical imaging, nir-ii, second near-infrared  

**Abstract**

Activatable molecular probes in the second near-infrared (NIR-II, 900−1700 nm) window are sought-after for in vivo optical imaging and biosensing. However, current molecular skeletons are confined to a limited range of dye systems, including cyanine, hemicyanine, and xanthene derivatives. Herein, we report a series of ortho-halogenated phenol-conjugated aza boron dipyrromethene platforms (OBDP-xOH, where X = H, F, Cl, Br, or 2Cl) that achieve bright NIR-II emission through a pH-regulated intramolecular charge transfer mechanism. The optimized scaffold, OBDP-2ClOH (pKa = 6.1), concurrently demonstrates the NIR-II emission maximum at approximately 905 nm, a large Stokes shift of 117 nm, high NIR-II brightness (εmaxΦf>1000 nm ≈ 38.46 M−1 cm−1), and excellent photochemical stability. To validate the broad applicability of this platform, we further designed three target-activatable NIR-II nanoprobes responsive to UV light, H2S, and Cu2+, respectively. Each nanoprobe exhibited a high fluorescence turn-on ratio exceeding 29-fold and limits of detection below 10.3 nM. Leveraging these probes, we achieved rapid (<30 min) dynamic imaging of labile Cu2+ in the brains of Parkinson’s disease models, as well as high-fidelity in vivo early detection of acute liver injury within 5 min, which is substantially faster than conventional clinical serum analysis that requires approximately 90 min. Collectively, these findings underscore the translational potential of the OBDP-2ClOH scaffold as a versatile and generalizable platform for the development of advanced activatable NIR-II probes.

### 52. [Longitudinal, Non-invasive Live Near-Infrared Fluorescence Imaging of Acute Vascularized Composite Allograft Rejection Validated by Ex Vivo 19F Magnetic Resonance Imaging and Histopathology](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/ae842a4a475a60e66deeb7a0bdba312ee780ca62)

**Authors:** Jelena M. Janjic, Y. Kulahci, F. Zor, S. Srishti, Riddhi Vichare, L. Foley, Zeynep Demir, Bedreddin Sazoglu, Fatma Nurefşan Selek, Omer Dirican, Vidya Surti, Stefan Stratimirovic et al.  
**Venue/category:** Nanotheranostics  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.7150/ntno.139245`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 24  
**Matched:** fluorescence imaging, near-infrared fluorescence, in vivo imaging  

**Abstract**

Acute rejection remains a major cause of graft attrition in vascularized composite allotransplantation (VCA). Clinical assessment and superficial biopsy may fail to detect early or spatially heterogeneous inflammatory infiltration, particularly within deeper graft compartments. Because macrophages are among the earliest mediators of alloimmune activation, they represent an attractive target for noninvasive immune surveillance of skin-bearing grafts. We evaluated longitudinal near-infrared fluorescence (NIRF) imaging of macrophage infiltration using a perfluoro-15-crown-5 ether nanoemulsion (PCE-NE) labeled with a near-infrared fluorophore in a rat hindlimb transplantation model and a radial forearm flap model in non-human primates (NHPs). Single- and dual-dose administration strategies enabled serial visualization of macrophage recruitment and improved detection of evolving inflammatory responses during acute rejection. To capture the spatial heterogeneity of rejection, a multi-sampled region-of-interest analysis framework was developed for quantitative mapping of NIRF signal across donor and recipient tissues in rats and throughout transplanted flap regions in NHPs. To validate in vivo imaging findings, grafts were analyzed ex vivo using fluorine-19 (19F) magnetic resonance imaging, histopathology, and CD68 immunofluorescence. NIRF signal identified rejecting graft regions and demonstrated spatial patterns of inflammatory infiltration that corresponded to compartment-specific 19F MRI signal localization in ex vivo imaging. Imaging findings corresponded with Banff rejection grade and CD68-positive macrophage accumulation across Grade I-IV rejection. Importantly, increased NIRF and 19F MRI signals were detected during early Grade I-II rejection, when clinical manifestations remained limited. These findings establish a translational multimodal imaging strategy for longitudinal, noninvasive monitoring of acute VCA rejection and demonstrate concordance between in vivo NIRF imaging, ex vivo 19F MRI, histopathology, and immunofluorescence across rodent and non-human primate models.

**Abstract**

Eumelanin has evolved from a biologically intriguing pigment into a functional material of growing interest for sustainable and biointegrated electronics. What makes eumelanin stand out is its rare combination of properties, including water-dependent ionic-electronic charge transport, redox activity, biocompatibility, and susceptibility to degradation. These features make it a hydration-programmable material capable of offering distinctive value instead of being a replacement for high-conductivity electronics. By linking chemistry and supramolecular hierarchy to transport, processing, and mechanics for sensors, transistors, soft energy devices, and biointerfaces, this review seeks to show how eumelanin can guide more sustainable material choices while creating opportunities for innovation.

### 55. [Antibody-Modified, Zwitterionic, and Fluorescent Polymeric Nanoparticles for Targeted Cellular Imaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/115d3f6f30efd7e66e06e410bfd9d275f2a2719f)

**Matched:** bioimaging, fluorescent bioimaging, fluorescent probe  

**Abstract**

Bright and specific fluorescent probes are key to the performance of fluorescent bioimaging and biosensing techniques, ranging from lateral flow assays to image-guided surgery. Fluorescently labeled antibodies are of particular interest as probes due to their high specificity. However, their labeling with molecular fluorophores is limited with respect to brightness due to self-quenching and a decrease of affinity. This can be overcome by using antibody-modified nanoparticles, yet in this case, the effect of NP size and surface chemistry has to be considered in detail. Here, we propose a streamlined assembly of bright antibody-modified stealth fluorescent nanoparticles. Our approach is based on the nanoprecipitation of zwitterion- and azide-bearing polymers together with a large amount of dye salts, followed by the coupling of the obtained nanoparticles to Trastuzumab through click chemistry. The size of the resulting nanoparticles was tuned between 15 and 30 nm. Labeling of HER2-expressing cells could be achieved, showing a clear increase in labeling intensity for antibody-modified nanoparticles compared to antibodies labeled with molecular dyes. Interestingly, the most efficient labeling was achieved with the smaller nanoparticles. The specificity of the labeling of cells with high versus low expression of HER2 through our antibody nanoparticles was shown directly in co-culture using multicolor imaging. The ease of assembly together with the high brightness and specificity makes the obtained nanoparticles attractive for the development of assays based on single-molecule detection and imaging.

### 56. [Phasor-based fluorescence lifetime imaging microscopy for label-free assessment of skin tissue responses to UV and 5G millimetre-wave exposure](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/93a9080bb6ecd44a8b5c8c7e067e23511f74b240)

**Authors:** Negin Foroughimehr, Andrew H. A. Clayton, Ali Yavari  
**Venue/category:** Journal of Optics  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.1088/2040-8986/aeb313`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 23  
**Matched:** optical imaging, fluorescence lifetime imaging, flim  

**Abstract**

Skin tissue is continuously exposed to a range of environmental stressors that can induce subtle biochemical and structural changes at the microscopic level. Consequently, there is growing interest in sensitive, non-invasive optical techniques capable of detecting these alterations for biomedical research and tissue characterisation. In this study, we investigated the utility of fluorescence lifetime imaging microscopy (FLIM) combined with phasor analysis as a label-free approach for detecting and differentiating tissue responses to environmental exposures. Porcine skin samples were subjected to ultraviolet (UV) radiation, 26 GHz fifth-generation (5G) millimetre-wave (mmWave) radiation, and a combined UV+mmWave exposure protocol. Following controlled irradiation, FLIM was employed to spatially map tissue fluorescence signatures and characterise exposure-induced changes within the skin microenvironment. Phasor-based analysis enabled visualisation and quantification of complex fluorescence lifetime distributions without the need for predefined fitting models, providing enhanced sensitivity to subtle tissue alterations. Distinct fluorescence lifetime patterns were observed among the exposure groups, with quantitative analysis revealing measurable differences in lifetime heterogeneity between UV-exposed, mmWave-exposed, and combined UV+mmWave-exposed tissues. The combined exposure group exhibited the greatest deviation from baseline fluorescence lifetime characteristics, while sunscreen-treated samples showed partial preservation of native fluorescence lifetime distributions, indicating a protective effect. Collectively, these findings demonstrate the potential of phasor-based FLIM as a sensitive optical imaging modality for detecting subtle microenvironmental changes in skin tissue and differentiating responses to multiple environmental stressors. The results further support the use of fluorescence lifetime biomarkers for label-free tissue characterisation and highlight the broader applicability of FLIM in biomedical optics, skin research, and environmental exposure assessment.

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar

**Abstract**

Heavy metal pollution poses a severe threat to ecosystems and human health. Therefore, it is important to develop rapid, sensitive, and reliable detection methods. Fluorescent probes with near-infrared (NIR, 650–900 nm) emission have emerged as powerful analytical tools owing to their distinct advantages, including low background interference, deep-tissue penetration, minimal photodamage, and high signal-to-noise ratios, which make them particularly attractive for both environmental monitoring and bioimaging applications. This review systematically summarizes recent progress in NIR organic small-molecule fluorescent probes for the detection of heavy metal ions, covering the period from 2022. Representative examples are categorized and discussed, with a focus on key metal ions such as Cu2+, Hg2+, Cd2+, Zn2+, Pb2+, Fe2+ and Fe3+. The molecular design strategies, sensing performance, and practical applications in environmental samples, food samples and biological imaging are critically discussed. Furthermore, current challenges and future opportunities in this rapidly advancing field are highlighted.
