**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 15  
**Matched:** biointegrated electronics, biointegrated  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT Flexible, self‐powered sensors are pivotal for next‐generation human‐machine interfaces. However, substrate‐induced clamping fundamentally restricts their electromechanical performance by suppressing longitudinal deformation, thereby reducing signal sensitivity. Here, we report a high‐performance freestanding PbZr0.52Ti0.48O3 (PZT) thin film based on a layered Mica/Pt/LaNiO3 heterostructure. The complete release of substrate constraints unlocks markedly enhanced properties, including a 1.4‐fold increase in remanent polarization (P r = 54 vs. 40 µC cm− 2), a 2.2‐fold enhancement in the effective converse piezoelectric coefficient (d 33 = 390 pm V−1 vs. 174 pm V−1), and an approximate 1.5‐fold improvement in dielectric permittivity. Consequently, the device exhibits rapid electromechanical transduction with an ultrashort pulse duration of 500 µs, alongside high output voltage and power. Integrated onto the human body, the sensor delivers sensitive, repeatable responses to various biomechanical motions. Beyond tactile sensing, the device enables high‐fidelity acoustic detection and intelligent signal analysis. By implementing harmonic evaluation and principal component analysis, the system achieves reliable discrimination among musical instruments with distinctive spectral fingerprints. Collectively, this work establishes the freestanding PZT film as a multifunctional platform integrating energy harvesting and intelligent acoustic recognition, offering new pathways for self‐powered biointegrated electronics.

### 284. [Bio-Inspired Transceivers for Molecular Communication: A Comprehensive Survey Bridging Communication Engineering, Synthetic Biology, and Nanotechnology](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/2e62fb756fb2c15f8d5f1bc4f44d0cc621760333)

**Authors:** Muhammad Usman Riaz, Lin Lin  
**Venue/category:** IEEE Internet of Things Journal  
**Date:** 2026-11-01  
**DOI:** `10.1109/jiot.2026.3717324`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, bioelectronic interface  

**Abstract**

The engineering of molecular communication (MC) systems has increasingly drawn inspiration from biology, leveraging insights from communication engineering, synthetic biology, and nanotechnology. Bio-inspired transceivers, synthetic constructs that emulate natural cellular signaling processes, offer a powerful foundation for realizing scalable, energy-efficient, and adaptive MC networks. To establish this foundation, we formalize key architectural attributes, including modularity, biocompatibility, signal fidelity, and energy efficiency, thus providing a generalized design framework. Building upon this framework, we examine biological systems, specifically neurons and bacteria, as archetypal models, mapping their native signaling processes to synthetic implementations of molecular transmitter (TX) and receiver (RX) modules. Complementing this biological perspective, we assess modeling techniques spanning stochastic, deterministic, and communication-theoretic domains, together with computational simulation frameworks. Furthermore, we survey application domains such as neurotherapeutics, bioelectronic interfaces, and targeted drug delivery, illustrating the practical relevance of bio-inspired transceivers. Finally, by highlighting open challenges and future opportunities, including metabolic optimization, adaptive interfaces, and regulatory integration, we outline a pathway toward the systematic design of intelligent, programmable, and biologically grounded MC transceivers.

### 285. [Microstructured Ionic Polymer-Based Multifunctional Iontronic Skin for Pressure Sensing, Object Recognition, and Underwater Environmental Monitoring](https://doi.org/10.1021/acsami.6c14386)

**Authors:** Shubham, Naman Patel, Hemant, Tanish Maheshwari, Dileep Kumar, Abhishek Kumar Singh, Vipin Amoli  
**Venue/category:** ACS Applied Materials & Interfaces  
**Date:** 2026-10-23  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acsami.6c14386`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** pedot:pss, iontronic  

**Abstract**

Abstract Skin-like iontronic sensors capable of multifunctional sensing and reliable operation hold strong potential for next-generation wearable electronics, human–machine interfaces, and underwater technologies. However, simultaneously achieving high sensitivity and multifunctionality within a single platform remains a significant challenge. Here, we report a multifunctional iontronic skin based on a microstructured ionic polymer (MSiP) composed of PVDF and [EMIM]+[BF4]– ion pairs. The MSiP is fabricated through mask-less photolithography to create highly ordered micropillar arrays with a diameter of 30 μm, a height of 5 μm, and an interspacing of 20 μm. The iontronic skin is constructed by sandwiching the MSiP layer between flexible PDMS/PEDOT:PSS electrodes, where pressure-induced migration of highly mobile [BF4]– ions, as revealed by molecular dynamics simulations, facilitates electrical double-layer formation at the MSiP/electrode interface, resulting in enhanced capacitive response. Moreover, finite-element simulations reveal that the microstructured architecture effectively concentrates local stress, increases the effective contact area, and amplifies structural deformation during compression, thereby accounting for the exceptional pressure sensitivity of the MSiP sensor. As a result, the sensor achieves an ultrahigh pressure sensitivity of 833 kPa–1, a highly linear response (R2 = 0.98), and excellent durability over 20,000 loading–unloading cycles. Its remarkable sensitivity enables the detection of pressures as low as 3 Pa. Furthermore, integration with a robotic arm enables the development of a tactile robotic interface platform capable of real-time object grasping, lifting, and releasing in response to tactile forces applied to the sensor surface. The sensor can also be attached to the human body to monitor physiological signals and various body movements. Beyond tactile sensing, when combined with machine learning, the sensor can accurately distinguish objects ranging from soft cloth to rigid steel bottles, achieving a high classification accuracy of 95–97%. The iontronic sensor also demonstrates underwater sensing, including monitoring of depth (0–35 cm), temperature (20–80 °C), and salinity (0–8 ppt). This work presents a versatile iontronic platform that enables high-performance multifunctional sensing for wearable electronics, intelligent robotics, human–machine interfaces, and underwater environmental monitoring.

### 286. [The Development and Evolution of Substrate Materials for Brain–Computer Interfaces](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/06da46eb456dfad910786b71cf96b509537de0d4)

**Authors:** Jiu-Jiang Ji, Yi-Dan Gao, Juan Liu, Mei Tian, Yen Wei  
**Venue/category:** Polymers  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.3390/polym18202569`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** neural interface, neural interfaces  

**Abstract**

Brain–Computer Interfaces (BCIs) hold tremendous potential for neurological therapeutics and neuroprosthetics; however, their long-term clinical viability is fundamentally limited by chronic interfacial failure. This failure originates from the profound biomechanical mismatch between synthetic microelectrodes and ultra-soft brain tissue, which inevitably triggers persistent mechanical trauma, severe foreign body response (FBR), and insulating glial scarring. In this review, we systematically trace the material and structural evolution of implantable neural interfaces designed to bridge this critical gap. We detail the chronological paradigm shift from rigid inorganic substrates (e.g., silicon) to flexible yet inextensible polymers (e.g., Polyimide, SU-8) for conformal mapping, and subsequently to intrinsically stretchable elastomers (e.g., polydimethylsiloxane polyurethane) that effectively absorb dynamic physiological micro-shear forces. Most importantly, we highlight the emerging frontier of electrospun nanofibrous microelectrodes. By uniquely combining macroscopic tissue-level stretchability with an interconnected, highly permeable 3D topography that mimics the native extracellular matrix (ECM), these biomimetic scaffolds successfully overcome the metabolic limitations of dense elastomeric films. Finally, we discuss current challenges and future perspectives, ranging from scalable high-density manufacturing and active biochemical modulation to fully untethered integration, charting a comprehensive roadmap for next-generation, chronically stable neural interfaces.

### 287. [AIEgen-Based Dual-Functional Platform for Cancer Cell Imaging and Photodynamic Therapy](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/01cfbaba3d244e54accfe4db0da88b050576187f)

**Authors:** Hirakjyoti Roy, Debika Barman, Ratul Chakraborty, Asis Bala, S. Ghosh, P. K. Iyer  
**Venue/category:** Langmuir  
**Date:** 2026-10-13  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.langmuir.6c05184`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** bioimaging, cell imaging  

**Abstract**

A new paradigm shift in translational healthcare research focuses on the development of a minimally invasive image-guided therapeutic strategy. The current study presents the design and synthesis of a series of donor–acceptor (D–A)-type aggregation-induced emission luminogens (AIEgens) specifically engineered for room temperature phosphorescence (RTP) emission and efficient photodynamic therapy. The newly synthesized AIEgen molecules incorporate an electron-rich thianthrene unit and a strong electron-withdrawing malononitrile moiety, forming a highly conjugated D–A system that promotes intramolecular charge transfer (ICT). The presence of bromine enhances spin–orbit coupling (SOC), enabling RTP emission and efficient intracellular reactive oxygen species (ROS) generation. The synthesized AIEgens exhibited strong emission in the aggregated state, which, upon light irradiation, generated elevated ROS levels, resulting in a significant dose-dependent reduction in cell viability. Among the three AIEgens evaluated, 2-BrTM showed the highest mitochondrial membrane depolarization of 48% and 30.1% upon light irradiation, compared to 7.57% and 3.43% in the dark condition for the breast cancer MCF-7 and MDA-MB-231 cells, respectively, demonstrating a pronounced photodynamic effect. Flow cytometric analysis confirmed that 2-BrTM induced early apoptosis, contributing to photoinduced cytotoxicity. The synthesized AIEgens exhibited efficient RTP with high quantum yields, yielding bright, long-lived emission suitable for bioimaging and cancer theranostics. In vivo mice studies further substantiated excellent biocompatibility of the compounds. Notably, this work demonstrated the potential of AIEgen-based RTP materials platforms for photodynamic therapy via ROS-mediated mitochondrial dysfunction and apoptosis.

### 288. [Large-scale all-optical electrophysiology links excitability and connectivity to circuit dynamics in vivo](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/fc1028bea83b02954a747f3835ca74f2529532dd)

**Authors:** Yitong Ǫi, Vicente J. Parot, Xiang Wu, Sarah E. Plutkis, Luke D. Lavis, Adam E. Cohen  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.05.756595`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** electrophysiology, voltage imaging  

**Abstract**

To understand neural computations, one would like to relate circuit dynamics to neuronal intrinsic excitability and connectivity. We developed tools for large-scale all-optical electrophysiology and applied them to approximately 300 layer 1 interneurons per mouse in vivo. Across two mice, targeted stimulation and voltage imaging identified 1,469 inhibitory and 5,752 directed electrical connections among 14,312 tested pairs. Presynaptic identity was more strongly associated with inhibitory post-synaptic potential (IPSP) amplitude, whereas postsynaptic identity was more strongly associated with IPSP decay time. Gap junctional and synaptic interactions had distinct spatiotemporal kernels, with corresponding signatures in spontaneous activity. Excitability measured under optogenetic stimulus predicted spiking responses to subthreshold voltage, depolarization rate, and spike history during spontaneous firing. Genetic subtypes also showed different behavioral-state-dependent voltage-to-spike relationships despite similar population-average subthreshold voltages. These measurements link cellular properties and pairwise interactions to spontaneous circuit dynamics, establishing an approach for functional circuit mapping in vivo. Highlights All-optical mapping links excitability, connectivity, and spontaneous dynamics in vivo Presynaptic and postsynaptic identity relate differently to IPSP strength and decay Gap junctional and synaptic couplings reveal distinct spatiotemporal interactions Measured intrinsic excitability predicts features of spontaneous spiking in vivo

### 289. [Scalable Fabrication of Chemically Stabilized Gelatin Hydrogel Fibers by Thermal Drawing](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/3e25b8efaec52c41640da7dd1ebda0da3e3bbb90)

**Authors:** Chao-Qun Dong, B. Schyrr, S. Laperrousaz, H. Banerjee, Andreas Leber, F. Sorin  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Materials Interfaces  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1002/admi.70702`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 14  
**Matched:** neural interface, neural interfaces  

**Abstract**

Gelatin represents a promising hydrogel fiber matrix for drug delivery, tissue engineering, and neural interfaces due to its inherent biocompatibility, biodegradability, and mechanical adaptability. Yet, existing soft‐material fabrication methods typically constrain fiber length, aspect ratio, throughput, and structural complexity, thereby restricting the capabilities of these fibers. Here, we introduce a multimaterial thermal drawing platform capable of continuously producing gelatin hydrogel fibers with lengths reaching tens of meters. Instead of relying on conventional synthetic crosslinkers, we leverage the Maillard reaction—adapted from food engineering—to induce in situ crosslinking under the heat generated during drawing. Guided by comprehensive rheological analyses, a moderate incubation step ensures a stable viscous flow regime essential for uninterrupted fiber production. This approach enables control over swelling and mechanical properties of hydrogel formulations, which exhibit elastic moduli in the tens to hundreds of kilopascals range and ultimate strains exceeding 100%. The hydrogel fibers can incorporate and gradually release therapeutic agents and generate low‐molecular‐weight species following collagenase exposure in vitro. By relying exclusively on naturally derived or naturally occurring constituents, the method circumvents the biocompatibility concerns associated with synthetic crosslinkers. The scalability and versatility of this fabrication platform open new design opportunities for hydrogel fibers tailored to diverse biomedical applications.

### 290. [Voice patches in the marmoset auditory cortex revealed by multimodal optical imaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/1cdb120e803d5e384f4365829e1e348561e6c5e7)

**Authors:** Yang Zhang, Chenggang Chen, Xin-Yan Song, Yue-Qi Guo, Xiao-Qin Wang  
**Venue/category:** Science Advances  
**Date:** 2026-10-21  
**DOI:** `10.1126/sciadv.aeh3885`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** optical imaging, calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Species-specific vocalizations (including human voices) are important for the survival and social interactions of both humans and animals. In both humans and nonhuman primates, voice/vocalization–selective regions have been identified on the lateral temporal cortex. Here, we provide evidence of localized and hierarchically organized voice patches in the auditory cortex of the common marmoset, a highly vocal New World primate, using multimodal optical imaging techniques that directly measure activity from the smooth cortical surface in a noise-controlled environment. Both intrinsic imaging and calcium imaging data revealed two discrete voice patches in a region of the auditory cortex. Our results provided precise functional localization of the two voice patches (posterior and anterior) by anchoring their positions to the tonotopic map and core/noncore areas of the auditory cortex. Both patches preferentially respond to marmoset vocalizations, exhibit quantifiable neural selectivity beyond spectral features, and encode call type and caller identity. Distinct response properties reveal functional differentiation between the posterior and anterior voice patches, with latency differences consistent with a hierarchical relationship. These findings provide new evidence for an evolutionarily conserved cortical architecture in primates that supports vocal communication via voice patches, analogous to face processing systems in human and nonhuman primate brains.

### 291. [Screw dislocation-mediated galaxy-mimicking superspiral assembly with amplified bio-piezoelectricity.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/52ac40a76286ed69783747380baccec1eb3281c5)

**Authors:** Jia-Hao Zhang, Hao-Ran Wu, Shi-Xuan Xu, Hao-Ye Jiang, Kang Ma, Syed A. M. Tofail, De-Qing Mei, Jun-Bai Li, Kai Tao

**DOI:** `10.1073/pnas.2611417123`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic  

**Abstract**

From the galaxies to the petals, spiral architectures are ubiquitous to confer hierarchical organizations, supporting asymmetric galactic evolutions and biological activities. Herein, inspired from nature, we report the superspiral architectures self-assembled by a minimalist amino acid. These superspirals, with spirality and geometry programmably tuned by enantiomeric excess and organization parameters, are determined to arise from the screw dislocation induced hierarchical crystallization. Notably, the spiral organization can integrate and amplify electrical polarization, yielding 17-fold enhancement in piezoelectric response contrast to random aggregates. This allows engineering of piezoelectric bioelectronics, resulting in high gain direction-selective motion sensing. Our work establishes an alternative paradigm for developing bioinspired hierarchical architectures of oriented and amplified properties, offering a self-assembling route to explore programmably modulated functional architectures for bioelectronic engineering.

### 293. [Tough Hydrogels for Remodeling Ruptured Tendons](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/0f0a62e4de0d5be1971286050fc023f7569f53fd)

**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.langmuir.6c05214`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic  

**Abstract**

Hydrogels have shown broad application potential due to their high water content and excellent interfacial adaptability, however, conventional biocompatible hydrogels often struggle to simultaneously achieve high strength, high toughness, and favorable biocompatibility, thereby limiting their practical applications. Developing new high-performance hydrogel systems capable of overcoming these mechanical limitations remains an important challenge. In this study, we developed a tough hydrogel that combines outstanding mechanical robustness with excellent biocompatibility. By introducing cyclic salt-solution training, dynamic reconstruction of the polymer network was induced, leading to the formation of a stable anisotropic architecture within the hydrogel. Binding-energy calculations support enhanced intermolecular association as a contributor to salt-assisted mechanical reinforcement. The tough hydrogel was further applied to the suture repair of ruptured rabbit tendons, where it maintained stable interfacial integration under complex dynamic in vivo conditions and promoted ordered collagen remodeling and functional tissue recovery. The tough hydrogel fabrication strategy proposed in this work provides a new approach for the development of high-performance hydrogels for applications in tissue repair, flexible bioelectronics, and advanced soft materials.

### 294. [Advances in Wound Closure Technologies: A Comprehensive Multi-Decadal Review](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/270bf5abd5125b494031b9617bece75b13310632)

**Authors:** Prathamesh Dhananjay Thorat, Shubhangi Dipak Narod, Payal Vasant Bhosale, Mansi Vikas Saudagar, Priyanka Anil Sanap, Deshmukh D. B, Unde R. S  
**Venue/category:** International Journal of Advanced Research in Science, Communication and Technology  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.48175/ijarsct-38866`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** bioelectronics, bioelectronic  

**Abstract**

Wound closure remains a cornerstone of surgical excellence and emergency medical intervention. Over the past several decades, the field has evolved from rudimentary mechanical approximation techniques to highly engineered, biomimetic, and intelligent theragnostic systems. This comprehensive review article details the historical trajectory, material science, biomechanical principles, and clinical outcomes of modern wound closure devices. We analyze traditional macro-mechanical benchmarks such as monofilament and multifilament sutures, mechanical staples, and hemostatic clips, alongside advanced non-invasive options including micro-anchor matrices and pressure-sensitive zip devices. Furthermore, we dissect the chemical kinetics and biological interactions of topical cyanoacrylate adhesives, fibrin glues, and catechol-functionalized hydrogels. Finally, we explore the cutting-edge frontier of smart closures equipped with flexible bioelectronics for real-time physiological monitoring and controlled drug-eluting capabilities. By assessing tensile strength, infection mitigation, cosmetic outcomes, and health economics, this review provides clinical practitioners and biomedical engineers with a unified framework for matching optimal closure modalities to specific anatomical and physiological demands. ### 295. [Simulation-guided design of a 3D flow focusing chip for the uniform production of polymer dots](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/07d3d51fb4620d404678e665b73e6f3c961af565)

**Authors:** R. C. K. Montalbo, Yung-Teng Chiu, Aishwarya Satpathy, Yang-Hsiang Chan, Hsiung-Lin Tu  
**Venue/category:** RSC Advances  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1039/d6ra08810d`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** conducting polymer, bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Semiconducting polymer dots (Pdots) are fluorescent conjugated polymer nanoparticles that exhibit high photoluminescence and biocompatibility, making them promising for high photoluminescence and biocompatibility, giving them high potential in biomedical applications such as bioimaging, drug delivery, and phototherapy. Accordingly, the size of the Pdots has become a critical factor in determining their bioavailability, biodistribution, and clearance. Towards this goal, microfluidic technologies have gained attention for the precise control of their size. However, the limitations of conventional glass capillary systems still inhibit the production of uniform and small nanoparticles. Here, computational fluid dynamics (CFD) simulations were used to guide the design of a 3D printed master for the fabrication of a solvent resistant 3D flow focusing microfluidic chip. Compared to glass capillary microfluidics, the developed chip offers more flexibility towards optimization of the design. Small and homogeneous Pdots (<50 nm) from commercially available MEH-PPV, CN-PPV, F8BT, and custom synthesized Pttc-4O-TTQ were produced. Large red shifts in the emission spectra of some Pdots suggest an efficient and tight packing of the polymers within the particles. Coprecipitation of the polymers with chitosan or PMSA enhanced their cell uptake, with complete cell uptake achieved in less than an hour in some cases. Furthermore, the spectral properties of both aggregation-induced quenching-prone and aggregation-induced emission-prone Pdots were observed, highlighting the chip's flexibility with different types of Pdots. The 3D flow focusing chip offers an accessible and cost-effective alternative to traditional microfluidic systems for the production of smaller Pdots.

### 296. [Catechol as a Co-Polymerization Mediator for Programmable Surface Chemistry Structure Synthesis of Carbon Dots.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/e1e64634b8f3cb43dc7135139a3b396890ac94ea)

**DOI:** `10.1002/anie.4229572`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** bioimaging, in vivo imaging  

**Abstract**

Red-emissive carbon dots (R-CDs) hold promise for bioimaging and optoelectronics, yet their synthesis under mild, scalable conditions remains challenging. Conventional hydrothermal routes demand high temperature and pressure, while ambient-pressure alternatives rely on strong acids and yield limited quantum yields without programmable surface control. This work reports a catechol-assisted ambient-pressure strategy that achieves hundred-gram-scale preparation of R-CDs by simple heating at ∼90°C. Catechol acts as a co-polymerization mediator, participating in formation of carbon dots while its dual hydrogen-bonding network suppresses functional-group detachment, stabilizes radical intermediates, and lowers the steric barrier for cyclization. This mechanism enables programmable tuning of hydrophilicity/hydrophobicity and emission wavelength by varying the precursor substituents. The hydrophobic carbon dots exhibit a solid-state quantum yield of 45.26% (encapsulated in polyvinylpyrrolidone), the highest reported, and enable white light-emitting diodes (WLED) with a color rendering index (CRI) of 94. The hydrophilic carbon dots (quantum yield 37.86%) are directly applied to deep-tissue in vivo imaging without surface modification. This work provides a versatile molecular engineering platform for sustainable synthesis of functional aromatic carbon nanomaterials.

### 297. [Bifunctional CuPt Nanoclusters and DSN-Mediated Target Recycling Enabled Dual-Mode OPECT/Colorimetric Biosensing for Ultrasensitive and Wide-Range Detection of miRNA-21](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/1e13ab2c2cf298634aeb446c79e8053a936f3eca)

**Authors:** Hong-Tao Chen, Xue Wang, Jia-He Chen, Qi-Qi Chao, De-Gang Jiang, Hong Zhou  
**Venue/category:** Analytical Chemistry  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.analchem.6c04513`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 13  
**Matched:** bioelectronic, electrochemical transistor  

**Abstract**

MicroRNA-21 (miRNA-21) is an important cancer biomarker that requires highly sensitive and reliable detection across a broad concentration range. Here, we report a dual-mode biosensing platform that integrates organic photoelectrochemical transistor (OPECT) amplification with nanozyme-assisted colorimetric transduction for ultrasensitive miRNA-21 analysis. By integrating a CdIn2S4/SnO2 heterojunction photogate, bifunctional CuPt nanoclusters (NCs), and duplex-specific nuclease (DSN)-driven target recycling, molecular recognition events are efficiently translated into two independent yet complementary analytical outputs. Upon target recognition, DSN-assisted cyclic cleavage triggers the accumulation of CuPt NC probes at the sensing interface, which simultaneously suppresses the photogating response through competitive light harvesting and interfacial charge-transfer regulation, while catalyzing the oxidation of TMB via their intrinsic peroxidase-like activity. Benefiting from the synergistic integration of transistor amplification, enzymatic target recycling, and dual-signal transduction, the dual-mode OPECT/colorimetric sensing platform achieves a detection limit of 0.072 fM with a wide range spanning from 0.1 fM to 100 nM, combined with excellent selectivity toward miRNA-21 detection in human serum samples. This work establishes a versatile dual-mode sensing framework that combines the high gain of bioelectronic transistors with the robustness of nanozyme-based colorimetry, offering new opportunities for nucleic acid analysis, early disease diagnosis, and point-of-care bioanalysis.

**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 12

**Abstract**

Abstract Stimuli-responsive prodrugs that combine therapeutic efficacy with optical reporting offer promising avenues for precision medicine. This study presents a dicyanoisophorone−naproxen (DCIP−Nap) conjugate, which merges the anti-inflammatory effects of naproxen with a donor−π−acceptor fluorescent scaffold. Initially, the conjugate is nonfluorescent due to suppressed intramolecular charge transfer. However, it shows a notable fluorescence “turn-on” response upon ester bond cleavage, facilitated by serum albumin. This selective release of active naproxen also restores the ICT process, resulting in a red-shifted emission. The probe further exhibits selective responsiveness toward peroxynitrite (ONOO−), a reactive nitrogen species abundantly generated in inflamed cells. Confocal microscopy using human glioblastoma (U-87 MG) and human leukemia monocytic (THP-1) cells demonstrated increased fluorescence intensity under inflammatory conditions, confirming ONOO−-mediated drug release. In vivo studies in LPS-treated mice showed fluorescence activation and decreased pro-inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α and IL-6), highlighting the therapeutic potential of DCIP−Nap. This study positions DCIP−Nap as an innovative fluorescent prodrug for targeted therapy and real-time monitoring in complex diseases.

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT The health of both individuals and ecosystems are seriously threatened by the heavy metal ion contaminant Pb 2+ . The traditional techniques for identifying Pb 2+ have a number of drawbacks like application to real sample analysis and in vivo bioimaging studies. Herein, an efficient chemosensor for the sensing of Pb 2+ , Zn 2+ and Cu 2+ ions has been produced belonging to imidazole‐oxadiazole cores. The sensing efficacy of 2‐(1‐(4‐(5‐(4‐(tert‐butyl)phenyl)‐1,3,4‐oxadiazol‐2‐yl)phenyl)‐4,5‐diphenyl‐1 H ‐imidazol‐2‐yl)‐4‐(diethylamino)phenol (DTB) was evaluated in acetonitrile:H 2 O medium. The probe DTB confirmed its extraordinary sensitivity concerning the metal ions Pb 2+ , Zn 2+ and Cu 2+ with very low limit of detection (LOD) of 29 nM for Pb 2+ , 21 nM for Zn 2+ and 33 nM for Cu 2+ . Unlike previously reported single‐core chemosensors, DTB uniquely integrates imidazole and 1,3,4‐oxadiazole motifs into a single fluorescent platform, enabling nanomolar detection of Pb 2 + , Zn 2 + and Cu 2 + together with intracellular metal‐ion imaging in zebrafish embryos.

**Abstract**

Abstract Advancements in spatial bioimaging analytics locate regions of interest as mask or vector geometries from raster images that are associated with molecular measurements, connectivity, and anatomical labels. A major challenge is to connect and jointly query these regions and their associated metadata at scale. The MicroData Model (muDM) is a spatial data model for associating 2D and 3D vector geometry with its annotated records, including identities, measurements, and hierarchies, as a precomputed multi-resolution pyramid. The data model has built-in integrations that emit target formats depending on use case, for consumption by machine learning and visualization applications. An open-source Python implementation is available on the Python Package Index, with a public catalog of over 100 datasets. In two Drosophila connectomes, the coarsest level retained at least 87% of finest-level neuron-typing accuracy with about 1% of the stored geometry. Linking synapse counts to the meshes improved accuracy beyond geometry alone.

### 308. [Band-Pass Fluorescence Activation via Dual-Responsive Molecular Design in the NIR-II Window for In Vivo pH Sensing](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/f9847fe24a4feb3680fa24a70fb2ec569aae493a)

**Authors:** Xin Zhang, Chun-Yan Liu, Bao-Feng Yun, Ben-Hao Li, Zu-Hai Lei, Meng-Yao Zhao, Fan Zhang  
**Venue/category:** Analytical Chemistry  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.analchem.6c04399`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** nir-ii  

**Abstract**

Real-time monitoring of physiological pH in living systems is essential for accurate disease evaluation and therapeutic guidance. However, conventional pH-responsive probes typically operate through monotonic activation and lack window selectivity, limiting their capacity to report further microenvironmental changes after crossing the activation threshold. Here, we report a NIR-II molecularly engineered band-pass pH (BPpH) sensing strategy that exhibits a non-monotonic “off-on-off” fluorescence response. By integrating an acid-responsive spirolactam switch with an alkaline-sensitive phenolic regulator, the BPpH probes enable window-restricted signal output within a defined pH range. BPpH-3 displays fluorescence activation from pH 3.6 to 5.2 and deactivation from pH 5.2 to 8.0, affording dual apparent pKa values of 4.16 and 7.42 and establishing a response window. Unlike conventional monotonic turn-on probes, this band-pass response enables continuous tracking of pH changes across the therapeutic process, and BPpH-3 further enables dynamic evaluation of therapeutic efficacy in a skin inflammation model, allowing timely assessment of treatment progression and dosing sufficiency. Moreover, its window-constrained response mitigates signal over-amplification under extreme acidification, facilitating improved tumor margin delineation for precision resection under NIR-II imaging guidance. The band-pass sensing strategy presented here provides a transferable framework for window-regulated detection of diverse physiological parameters and holds promise for integration with multiple diagnostic imaging modalities.

### 309. [Peptidomimetic Polymer With Conformation-Coded Hierarchical Assembly for Visualizing Biodistribution.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/4b9e51fef86307b8c2b8075b3804952ebcda4e28)

**Authors:** Ye-Qiang Zhou, Shuang-Yan Li, Dan Yang, Yi-Wei Wang, J. Zou, Ming-Hao Wang, Chuan Peng, Yang Liu, Hong Tan, Ming-Ming Ding  
**Venue/category:** Angewandte Chemie  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1002/anie.1895737`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging, optical imaging  

**Abstract**

Directly encoding local physicochemical information into an intrinsically self-referenced fluorescence signal remains a major challenge in bioimaging. Here, we report an aliphatic peptidomimetic polymer system whose luminescence is programmed through conformation-directed hierarchical assembly. β-sheet-like and random-coil conformations direct self-assembly into rigid nanotubes and isotropic vesicles, respectively, with distinct emission profiles. At elevated local concentrations, the nanotubes undergo higher-order assembly into spherical superstructures exhibiting a J-aggregate-like red-shifted optical response, with a pronounced blue-to-orange color transition and emission extending into the near-infrared region. We exploit this intrinsic concentration-dependent color response for multicolor organelle imaging in living cells and for noninvasive, visually interpretable, semiquantitative mapping of polymer biodistribution in living animals. By converting local concentration into emission hue rather than absolute intensity, this work establishes a new design principle for self-referenced optical imaging, with potential applications in biosensing, quantitative bioimaging, and drug delivery.

### 310. [napari-TurboBox: Interactive Multi-Viewer Bounding Box Annotation for AI-Guided Volumetric Bioimaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/3195c49a106ce2b8eca027ef72e7c4829fbb3e06)

**Authors:** Nils Friederich, Philip Kohle, M. Beichter, Mona Wellhäusser, Marcel Stahl, Pascal Zwick, Angelo Jovin Yamachui Sitcheu, Thomas Lautenschlager, David Exler, Linus Schilpp, Stephan Allgeier, Andreas Specker et al.  
**Venue/category:** eess.IV  
**Date:** 2026-10-14  
**arXiv:** `2610.22413`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar, arXiv  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Promptable segmentation models turn one 3D bounding box into an instance mask. Box placement therefore limits how fast microscopy volumes with thousands of objects are annotated. Each box must be checked in several synchronized viewers, but napari-bbox shares no state between viewers. An edit must update every viewer and slows down with more boxes. napari-TurboBox instead holds one shared copy of the boxes for all viewers. In a four-viewer layout, napari-TurboBox needs a few milliseconds per mouse move to update the three slice viewers up to $10^4$ boxes. The same update in napari-bbox leaves the 33 ms budget beyond about $10^3$ boxes and takes 159 ms at $10^4$. Boxes stay axis-aligned and export to COCO and YOLO as per-slice 2D boxes. napari-TurboBox is a pure-Python plugin for napari 0.6.6 to 0.9. The code is available at https://github.com/N-Friederich/napari-turbobox under the Apache 2.0 licence, and version 0.1.0 is archived at https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23090828 on Zenodo.

### 311. [High‐Efficiency Near‐Infrared Forsterite Mg 2 SiO 4 : Cr 3+ , Yb 3+ Phosphor for Optical Bioimaging and Night Vision Applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/1492ddf45e75a6714e7c1452e44e9b49c081c7cc)

**Authors:** Jing Yan, Lei Zhong, Yue-Fei Xiang, Hui-Yao Zhu, You-Wang Long, Chun-Yan Jiang, Ming-Mei Wu  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Optical Materials  
**Date:** 2026-10-13  
**DOI:** `10.1002/adom.71913`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT The advancement of near‐infrared phosphor‐converted light‐emitting diodes (NIR pc‐LEDs) for next‐generation optoelectronic applications is critically dependent on the availability of high‐performance phosphors. In this study, a series of NIR‐emitting phosphors with high luminescence efficiency and excellent stability was developed by co‐doping Cr 3+ , Yb 3+ into the Mg 2 SiO 4 (MSO) host. Efficient energy transfer from Cr 3+ to Yb 3+ was achieved in the MSO: Cr 3+ , 2.5%Yb 3+ phosphor, reaching an energy transfer efficiency of 91.82%, as determined from the luminescence intensity measurement. The optimal MSO: Cr 3+ , 1.5%Yb 3+ phosphor exhibits a remarkable internal quantum efficiency (IQE) of 71.8% and an external quantum efficiency (EQE) of 48.8%. It also demonstrates remarkable thermal stability, retaining 71.5% of its initial intensity at 423 K. Additionally, the material shows exceptional humidity resistance, retaining approximately 98.05% of its initial intensity after 7 days of exposure to harsh conditions (85°C and 85% relative humidity, RH). Notably, no significant chromaticity shift is observed at elevated temperatures, indicating excellent color stability. Finally, an NIR pc‐LED was fabricated by combining the optimized phosphor with a 450 nm commercial blue LED chip. The device delivers a high output power of 718.67 mW with an electro‐optical conversion efficiency of 12.53% at a driving current of 600 mA. Collectively, these results underscore the promising potential of this phosphor for applications in optical bioimaging and night vision.

### 314. [Model-agnostic Knowledge Augmented Machine Learning for Weakly Annotated Bioimaging Data](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/fe953e2c9b41893922655e572fa0799aac08551c)

**Authors:** Jayanie Bogahawatte, Sachith Seneviratne, Simon Maksour, Dzung Do-Ha, Elizabeth Qian, S. Brennan, Matt Keon, L. Ooi, Saman K. Halgamuge  
**Venue/category:** eess.IV  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**arXiv:** `2610.13637`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar, arXiv  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Machine learning models are increasingly used for automated feature extraction from weakly annotated bioimaging data, such as microscopy and cytomorphology images. Due to the sole dependency of automated feature extraction, existing methods yield inconsistent classification performance across weakly annotated datasets. Here, we propose a model-agnostic knowledge augmentation method that enables ML models to exploit domain knowledge beyond what automated feature extraction can capture. The proposed approach improves classification performance across seven ML architectures. When integrated with masked autoencoder-based self-supervised learning, our method achieves the strongest overall performance, improving precision by 2.9% over the next-best method on three in-house microscopy datasets and by an average of 6.8% across three public cytomorphology benchmarks. We perform nucleus-level classification based on mislocalization of TAR DNA-binding protein 43 (TDP-43) from the nucleus to the cytoplasm, a phenotype linked to Amyotrophic Lateral Sclerosis (ALS), using three independent microscopy datasets of motor neurons derived from human induced pluripotent stem cells. Accurate quantification of this phenotype at scale remains challenging and may contribute to inconsistencies across studies. Application of the best-performing model to these microscopy datasets reveals biological interpretations related to ALS, including dose-dependent effects of the proteasomal stressor, MG132, and the oxidative stressor, sodium arsenite (SA), on TDP-43 mislocalization, variable pathology in motor neurons from sporadic and familial ALS donors, and a lack of protection of the pharmacological compounds, edaravone and niclosamide, against SA-induced TDP-43 mislocalization. Together, our pipeline enables in-vitro cellular image analysis to advance understanding of ALS-associated pathology and potential treatments.

### 315. [In vivo two-photon imaging of vascular response to laser-induced shockwaves](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/bba9150b76334f78a6e3da6587cff659064f5765)

**DOI:** `10.1117/12.3113255`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** two-photon imaging  

**Abstract**

We present an experimental approach for in vivo investigation of laser-induced shockwave interactions with microvasculature using two-photon intravital microscopy. A nanosecond 1030-nm laser beam was co-aligned with the two-photon imaging axis and delivered through a high-numerical-aperture objective to generate localized photomechanical perturbations near superficial blood vessels in the mouse ear, labeled with FITC-Dextran for high-resolution multiphoton visualization. Two-photon imaging was used to monitor changes in vessel morphology and fluorescence distribution as indicators of vascular response. This work establishes a compact experimental framework for correlating controlled laser-induced mechanical excitation with intravital imaging, providing a foundation for future studies of shockwave–vascular interactions and optically mediated biomechanical effects in tissues.

### 316. [DeepFOCUS: deep learning-enhanced Fourier-domain intensity coupling enables in vivo hippocampal two-photon imaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/9317a3f428e6ae05c565c2710136a87b3ea61480)

**Authors:** Yi Xue  
**Venue/category:** Emerging Topics in Artificial Intelligence (ETAI) 2026

**DOI:** `10.1117/12.3114853`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** two-photon imaging  

**Abstract**

Multiphoton microscopy is widely used for deep-tissue imaging, but its imaging depth remains limited by tissue scattering. We recently developed Fourier-domain intensity coupling for scattering correction (FOCUS), which enables imaging through the entire cortex and white matter in the mouse brain in vivo. To achieve faster and more effective scattering correction, we developed DeepFOCUS, which uses deep learning to generate Fourier-domain correction masks for FOCUS. DeepFOCUS enables in vivo two-photon imaging of YFP-labeled neurons in the hippocampus of the intact mouse brain. Compared with uncorrected two-photon imaging, DeepFOCUS increases fluorescence intensity by tens of fold at the same post-objective power.

### 317. [Bioimaging at the photon limit](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/08fa2d43a19fb116030b7a8c6a3bc845e11b5da3)

**Authors:** S. Balogun, Lucas Sheneman, Demetrios N. Christodoulides, A. Vasdekis

**DOI:** `10.1117/12.3118621`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Fluorescence microscopy has transformed the study of living systems, but its reliance on high excitation irradiance can perturb the very biological processes it seeks to observe. This limitation is especially pertinent to organisms, cells, and molecular pathways adapted to dim or light-sensitive environments. Here, we describe a photon-efficient microscopy platform that can integrate light-sheet imaging, amplified low-light detection, and a unified framework of model-based and deep-learning reconstruction to image biological specimens under extreme photon sparsity. By fusing hardware photon collection with computational inference, the approach extracts deterministic spatial structure from discrete photon measurements. Together, these advances enable imaging at irradiance levels approximately 10,000-fold lower than those used in standard high-performance fluorescence microscopy, opening a path toward live-cell observation under conditions that truly preserve native physiology.

### 318. [Biogenic Eu 2 O 3 Nanostructures Exhibiting Visible–Near-Infrared Luminescence for Bioimaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/c947eb5b45fe724dfefb7ce3ec712c1bebc68963)

**Authors:** Marimuthu Venkatesh Perumal, G. Kanthimathi

**DOI:** `10.1142/s2251237326500115`  
**Source(s):** Crossref, Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

This study reports a facile and ecofriendly synthesis of europium sesquioxide (Eu 2 O 3 ) nanoparticles using Dragon Blood Resin (Daemonorops draco Blume) extract as a natural agent. The nanoparticles were characterized by FTIR, XRD, FE-SEM with EDS, UV-Vis, diffuse reflectance spectroscopy (DRS), and photoluminescence spectroscopy. FTIR confirmed Eu-O vibrational modes (528-858 cm −1 ), while XRD of the 800℃ product revealed a cubic structure with an average crystallite size of 28 nm (the 500℃ product was only partially amorphous). FE-SEM showed spherical morphology, and EDS composition was consistent with Eu 2 O 3 . Optical bandgap analysis using Tauc plots derived from UV-Vis and DRS measurements yielded bandgap values of approximately 4.1 and 3.85 eV, respectively, consistent with the wide-bandgap semiconducting nature of Eu 2 O 3 nanoparticles. Photoluminescence spectra showed features near 504 nm (255 nm excitation) and 786 nm (395 nm excitation); as these wavelengths lie close to twice the respective excitation wavelengths, second-order diffraction or scattered-excitation artefacts cannot currently be excluded, and confirmatory measurements (order-sorting filters, excitation-wavelength dependence, fluorescence lifetime) are needed before these features can be attributed to genuine Eu 2 O 3 photoluminescence. Preliminary confocal imaging of treated cells in the blue-green-red range showed luminescence signals consistent with cellular association, though cytotoxicity, vehicle controls, biological replicates, and internalization-specific imaging remain to be established before a bioimaging application can be considered validated.

### 319. [Artificial intelligence for microscopy: from image analysis to intelligent imaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/dea3750457a95a4ccd9c7d4794c75da7e210d0f8)

**Authors:** A. Muñoz-Barrutia  
**Venue/category:** Emerging Topics in Artificial Intelligence (ETAI) 2026  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.1117/12.3117353`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 12  
**Matched:** bioimaging, optical imaging  

**Abstract**

Recent advances in microscopy are generating unprecedented volumes of high-resolution biological data across spatial and temporal scales. Artificial intelligence is rapidly transforming how this data is interpreted, enabling the automated extraction of quantitative information from complex images and opening new opportunities for data-driven discovery in biology and medicine. This lecture will discuss recent progress in the integration of deep learning with microscopy workflows, highlighting how AI methods are reshaping the analysis of biological images and enabling reproducible, scalable computational pipelines. Particular emphasis will be placed on the development of accessible AI tools and open computational frameworks that allow researchers to deploy advanced machine-learning models directly within established bioimaging environments. Interdisciplinary approaches that combine AI-based image analysis with physical and biological modeling are also emerging as powerful strategies to study complex biological systems. Such approaches enable quantitative investigation of cell mechanics, tissue organization, and large-scale three-dimensional histological reconstruction, illustrating how computational methods can reveal new insights into biological structure and function across scales. Finally, emerging directions at the intersection of artificial intelligence and optical imaging will be discussed, including smart microscopy systems capable of adaptive acquisition, agent-based AI approaches for scientific discovery, and the growing importance of sustainable computing in data-intensive bioimaging.

---

### 320. [Enzyme-Activatable Probe for Tumor Bimodal Imaging, Intraoperative Navigation, and Urinalysis.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/19472f27b700ea6a6e41e7c0be284c33f8f8cb44)

**Abstract**

We develope an enzyme-activatable platform (NO2-RGD-Gd) for the integrated urinary biomarker detection, highcontrast fluorescence-magnetic resonance (MR) dualmodal imaging, and real-time image-guided surgical navigation within a single molecule. The advantages, including the favorable tumor-targeting affinity (RGD peptide), high renal clearance efficiency, and tumor-specific fluorescence activation, are achieved by rationally engineering the molecular architecture. Benefiting from these characteristics, the NO2-RGD-Gd effectively bridges the boundary between diagnosis and therapy, achieving outstanding combinatorial performance in in vivo applications. It allows early and precise diagnosis through urinary biomarkers, provides anatomical-pathological and molecular imaging information of tumor lesions via fluorescence-MR dual-modal imaging, and delineates tumor boundaries for accurate surgical navigation relying on tumor-targeted activatable fluorescence imaging signals. Furthermore, all these processes can be realized simply via intravenous injection, offering a more efficient and feasible strategy for integrated diagnostic and therapeutic applications.

### 321. [Physics-Guided Neural Phase Demodulation for Non-Contact Laser-Ultrasound Sensing of Corneal Phantom Deformation](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/6746eaf79b10e25fd011823e3859d223dbea22fe)

**Authors:** Muhammad Awais, Younggue Kim, B. Lee  
**Venue/category:** Biosensors  
**Date:** 2026-10-13  
**DOI:** `10.3390/bios16100577`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 11  
**Matched:** biosensor, bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Non-contact detection of weak, transient acoustic signals is essential for photoacoustic and laser-ultrasound (LUS) bioimaging, including the assessment of corneal deformation and thickness variation. Passive 3 × 3 fiber-coupler interferometers provide phase-diverse sensing without external modulation; however, conventional ellipse-based calibration becomes unreliable when the measured phase excursion covers only a small fraction of the characteristic ellipse, as in nanometer-scale corneal responses. This study presents a physics-guided temporal convolutional network (PG-TCN) that directly maps two nonideal interferometric outputs to a continuous dynamic phase signal without ellipse fitting or separate phase unwrapping during inference. The model combines dilated one-dimensional convolutions with interferometric-consistency and temporal-regularization losses. Tests using PZT-modulated calibration signals acquired under several static chamber pressures of 10, 15, 20, and 25 kPa showed lower mean absolute error, higher normalized cross-correlation, and lower displacement RMSE than a CNN-LSTM baseline. In corneal-phantom LUS measurements, the first detected echo shifted from 4.632 to 4.488 µs between 10 and 20 kPa, corresponding to a relative thickness reduction of approximately 7.8 µm/kPa. The PG-TCN required 210 ± 18 µs per sequence while using approximately half the trainable parameters of the CNN-LSTM baseline. These results demonstrate a fast physics-guided optical detection framework for non-contact sensing of weak acoustic responses, supporting the use of 3 × 3 optical interferometry as a biosensing approach for LUS and photoacoustic applications.

---

### 322. [Mitochondrial apoptosis imaged by high-throughput confocal microscopy](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/b30eb2d2d7ec40e9fbd659e982fe57228ea08af3)

**Authors:** Lina Hellwig, Felix J. Meigel, H. Stachelscheid, S. Rulands, P. Mergenthaler  
**Venue/category:** Scientific Data  
**Date:** 2026-10-15  
**DOI:** `10.1038/s41597-026-08502-7`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 10  
**Matched:** cell imaging, live-cell imaging  

**Abstract**

Mitochondrial programmed cell death (i.e. intrinsic apoptosis) is regulated through the formation of pro-apoptotic protein complexes at the mitochondrial outer membrane (MOM), which permeabilize the MOM and commit the cell to die. This step is modulated by mitochondrial dynamics. To capture this, we treated cells with the mitochondrial dynamics inhibitor M1 before inducing apoptosis with actinomycin D (ActD), and imaged them by high-throughput spinning-disk confocal microscopy. In mouse embryonic fibroblasts (MEFs) we followed apoptosis by long-term live-cell imaging, and in human induced pluripotent stem cells (hiPSCs) we stained fixed cells for the pro-apoptotc protein BAX at mitochondria. Here, we provide a description of these two large-scale microscopy datasets. Dataset 1 contains 637,632 images of MEFs imaged as time series after labelling of mitochondrial morphology and integrity with the mitochondrial membrane potential-sensitive dye tetramethylrhodamine-ethylester (TMRE) and apoptosis with Annexin V-FITC. The dataset also contains a digital phase contrast channel which can be useful to track cells. Dataset 2 contains 9,504 images of hiPSCs with immunofluorescence labelling of BAX and COX IV. The datasets are made available through the BioImage Archive repository.

### 323. [Safe and efficient intracellular delivery of functional antibodies into cultured neural cells shows a role for Alzheimer’s-relevant tau phosphorylation in cell integrity](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/ca7a5afe281deab837fa307e58273c6d4f3a9034)

**Authors:** K. Viola, B. A. Chromy, Raghad Nowar, M. Lambert, William L. Klein  
**Venue/category:** Frontiers in Dementia  
**Date:** 2026-10-13  
**DOI:** `10.3389/frdem.2026.1737082`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 10  
**Matched:** cell imaging, live-cell imaging  

**Abstract**

Protein proteoforms generated by post-translational modification are increasingly recognized as important regulators of neuronal function and neurodegenerative disease. However, methods for acutely interrogating specific intracellular proteoforms in living neuronal populations remain limited. We evaluated cationic liposomal delivery (CLD) as a strategy for introducing full-length functional antibodies into living neurons and used this approach to investigate the role of a phosphorylated tau proteoform recognized by the monoclonal antibody PHF-1. Cationic liposomes composed of DOPC+ were complexed with antibodies or molecular probes and applied to cultured neuronal cell lines and primary mouse cortical neurons. Intracellular delivery, cellular localization, viability, and biological activity were assessed using fluorescence microscopy and live-cell imaging. CLD efficiently delivered full-length antibodies and molecular probes into living neuronal cells and primary neurons without detectable toxicity. Internalized antibodies retained target specificity, producing intracellular staining patterns consistent with the known localization of tau, vinculin, paxillin, and Fyn. Delivery was observed across essentially all cells in treated cultures and remained effective under standard culture conditions. Intracellular delivery of PHF-1 produced a rapid and striking morphological response characterized by neurite retraction and cell-body rounding within hours of treatment. These effects were not observed with heat-inactivated PHF-1, vehicle controls, or other intracellular antibodies, supporting a specific effect of intracellular engagement of PHF-1-reactive tau species. These findings establish CLD as a practical platform for the intracellular delivery of functional full-length antibodies into living neuronal populations. Beyond validating a delivery technology, the study demonstrates that acute intracellular engagement of PHF-1-reactive phosphorylated tau rapidly alters neuronal morphology, suggesting that this tau proteoform contributes to the maintenance of neurite integrity. CLD provides a versatile approach for probing the function of intracellular proteoforms that are difficult to study using conventional genetic or biochemical methods.

---

### 324. [RNDC-Lab: a real-time calcium imaging-based adaptive neural decoding framework](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/45fb594bf79f104b154791d42b07bff0a8599e2f)

**Authors:** Kavinda Kehelella, Xiao-Min Wu, Hamza Ali, Chen-Wei Yang, Jing Xie, Yaesop Lee, Rong Chen, Shuvra S. Bhattacharyya  
**Venue/category:** Frontiers in Neuroscience  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.3389/fnins.2026.1902832`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 10  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Real-time neural decoding is critical for precise neuromodulation, which is of increasing interest in the treatment of brain disorders. This paper presents RNDC-Lab, a computational framework for designing, optimizing, and experimenting with real-time, calcium imaging-based, end-to-end neural decoding systems. RNDC-Lab integrates calcium imaging preprocessing with adaptive neural decoding using compact Deep Neural Networks (DNNs). RNDC-Lab supports dynamic retraining of a neural decoding model concurrently with inference, thereby providing robustness to changes in the statistical properties of input calcium imaging signals. We integrate NDSEP and NeuroGRS — two recently- developed tools for neural decoding and signal extraction, and optimized generation of DNN models, respectively—into RNDC-Lab. This results in the first real-time neural decoding system based on calcium imaging with a dynamic, DNN-based decoding model. This paper also introduces a number of simulated datasets to assist in system evaluation and experimentation using RNDC-Lab, and more broadly, as resources for experimentation with arbitrary neural decoding models and systems. The experimental capabilities of RNDC-Lab are further enhanced by four new datasets—the Brain Phantom, N80, N90 and Reward Learning (for multiclass classification) datasets—that we introduce in this work as companion resources for working with RNDC-Lab. To demonstrate the capabilities of RNDC-Lab, we conduct extensive experiments to evaluate the functionality and performance of the proposed framework. The experiments incorporate the aforementioned simulated datasets as well as real calcium imaging data collected from mouse models. Our results demonstrate that the RNDC-Lab system, combined with NDSEP and NeuroGRS, achieves efficient and accurate real-time neural decoding from both real and synthetic calcium imaging data. Moreover, the results demonstrate RNDC-Lab's capability for dynamic model retraining and characterize the associated trade-offs involving decoding performance and execution time. While the components within NDSEP, NGSynth, and NeuroGRS are used to concretely demonstrate the capabilities of RNDC-Lab in this paper, the rigorous dataflow- based architecture of RNDC-Lab enables these components to be selectively replaced with alternative modules that provide the same general functionality using different algorithms or implementations (e.g., a new module for motion correction or model retraining). This makes RNDC-Lab a powerful tool for experimenting with novel aspects of neural decoding in the context of an overall real-time framework.

**Abstract**

The growing demand for rapid, accurate, and decentralized diagnostics has accelerated the development of point-of-care (PoC) biosensors for early disease detection. In this work, we demonstrate the detection of the HIV-specific capsid antigen p24 protein by a compact, fully electronic, and reconfigurable bioelectronic platform based on impedance sensing. The platform integrates a multichannel array of gold interdigitated electrodes (IDEs) that can be independently functionalized to enable multiplexing, redundancy, and statistical validation. A differential readout architecture, combining transimpedance amplification with FPGA-based digital lock-in detection, ensures the highest stability over temperature and salinity fluctuations and effective rejection of common-mode errors. Experimental validation demonstrated sensitive detection of HIV p24 across multiple concentrations, with a limit of detection (LOD) of 8.5 pg/mL. Measurements on virus isolate samples further confirmed the robustness and reliability of the platform. These results establish the system as a versatile and scalable option for next-generation PoC diagnostics, with particular strengths in stable, multichannel, and adaptable antigen detection: thanks to the copolymer coating of the chips, reconfiguration requires only the substitution of the spotted probes, enabling applications that range from highly specific biorecognition elements to broader capture molecules.

### 332. [A near-infrared fluorescent probe for imaging hydrogen peroxide in living cells and acute liver injury.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/a8212942e47dfa6047544b2d7c7f2a8d8443a0e3)

**Abstract**

Acute liver injury (ALI) is linked to oxidative stress, with hydrogen peroxide H2O2 as a key mediator. We report a near-infrared fluorescent probe, PhCy-H2O2, based on an ICT mechanism. The probe shows high sensitivity (detection limit in the low micromolar range) and excellent selectivity, producing a significant fluorescence turn-on at 796 nm upon H2O2 recognition. This reaction has the advantages of high sensitivity, high selectivity, fast reaction and stability under physiological pH conditions. In both cellular and mice models, the probe clearly visualized dose-dependent H2O2 generation induced by LPS and APAP, with fluorescence signals effectively attenuated by GSH and NAC, respectively, confirming the observed signals are associated with oxidative stress. Moreover, the hepatic fluorescence intensities correlated well with the levels of MDA and TNF-α, and the signal changes faithfully reflected the protective effects of antioxidant interventions, indicating that the probe reliably reports the severity of oxidative injury in ALI. Thus, PhCy-H2O2 offers a reliable tool for studying oxidative stress in ALI and evaluating hepatoprotective agents.

### 333. [A Bilayer Bioelectronic Implant with Piezoelectric‐Driven Immunomodulation and Bioactive Whitlockite for Bone Regeneration](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/7affcf01bbc9f8f29196f70bae01066bbfc502b5)

**Authors:** Qi-Rui Gong, Zhi-Han Zhang, Guang-Xu Wang, Xuan Yu, Li-Hong Fan, Tian-Tian Chen, Hui-Xuan Sun, Xiao Zheng, Sheng-Xiang Tao, Ying-Ying Chu, Yue Zhang  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Functional Materials

**DOI:** `10.1002/adfm.79007`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 9  
**Matched:** bioelectronic  

**Abstract**

The loss of endogenous electrical signals after bone injury, coupled with infection and oxidative stress, often impedes natural healing. Here, we develop a bilayered scaffold integrating a piezoelectric nanofibrous membrane with a conductive antioxidative hydrogel for coordinated antibacterial defense, immunomodulation, and bone regeneration. The top layer comprises electrospun P(VDF‐TrFE) doped with polarized whitlockite (PPW), generating local electrical potentials under mechanical or mild ultrasonic stimulation. The bottom layer consists of lignin‐stabilized silver nanoparticles embedded in a polyacrylamide network (ALP), providing broad‐spectrum antibacterial activity and ROS‐scavenging in vitro. In vitro, the piezoelectric signals enhanced ALP activity and collagen I expression by 1.35‐fold, while the ALP hydrogel drove macrophage polarization from M1 toward M2. In a rat bilateral 3‐mm full‐thickness calvarial defect model, the ultrasound‐stimulated scaffold increased bone volume fraction 2.3‐fold, improved new bone formation and partial cortical bridging, and nearly doubled VEGF expression at 4 weeks relative to the ALP hydrogel group, though residual defects remained at 8 weeks. Concurrently, the scaffold upregulated TGF‐β and Runx2 while markedly suppressing TNF‐α, indicating an accelerated transition from inflammation to repair. These results demonstrate a synergistic strategy in which piezoelectric stimulation and biochemical protection jointly orchestrate osteogenesis, angiogenesis, and immunomodulation for bone tissue engineering.

### 334. [Comparative Evaluation of Aptamer- and Antibody-Functionalized Gold Surfaces for Bacillus Cereus Spore Capture](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/9aa3e493e7d27c803c2a19566572aa24d9b98708)

**Matched:** biosensor, fluorescence imaging  

**Abstract**

The development of an effective recognition interface is a critical step in biosensor design. In this study, aptamer- and antibody-functionalized gold surfaces were compared as capture interfaces for Bacillus cereus spores. Two aptamer sequences and different antibody immobilization approaches, including random and oriented immobilization, were investigated. Spore capture was evaluated by fluorescence imaging using either an aptamer–antibody sandwich assay or direct SYTO®9 staining, with Raman spectroscopy employed as a complementary characterization method. At the fixed spore concentration investigated, the aptamer-functionalized surfaces produced higher fluorescence responses than the antibody-functionalized surfaces under the tested experimental conditions. Raman bands associated with calcium dipicolinate provide additional evidence consistent with the presence of captured endospores. An opal/gold hybrid structure was also examined as a preliminary optical transduction platform. The immobilization of two distinct molecular recognition elements (MREs) was performed via spotter deposition, thus enhancing the signal-to-noise ratio. Our findings suggest that, in this configuration, aptamers outperform antibodies in capturing B. cereus spores and, therefore, are identified as the most useful MRE to be implemented in future biosensors. Furthermore, an easy method for the fluorescence labeling of spores, i.e., via SYTO®9, is proposed as an effective way to directly detect spore presence.

### 335. [The multifaceted role of SsrB in S. Typhi H58 during THP-1 macrophage infection](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/ed82e700d58e0f4a0e58b175c30a18750a67f709)

**Authors:** Marion Fernandez, Soham Deolankar, Moirangthem Kiran Singh, Parisa Zangoui, Linda J. Kenney  
**Venue/category:** Frontiers in Microbiology  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.3389/fmicb.2026.1849419`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 9  
**Matched:** fluorescence lifetime imaging  

**Abstract**

S . Typhi, the causative agent of typhoid fever, remains less well understood due to its human-restricted nature compared to S . Typhimurium, which enjoys a broad host range. Herein, we investigated the acid response of S . Typhi and the role of the SPI-2 master regulator SsrB during infection of human THP-1 macrophages. Using fluorescence lifetime imaging of the pH-sensitive probe mCherryTYG, we demonstrate that the S . Typhi cytosol acidifies during infection of THP-1 macrophages, similar to S . Typhimurium. This acidification triggers a marked up-regulation of SsrB, with S . Typhi expressing significantly more SsrB molecules than S . Typhimurium, as quantified by photoactivatable localization microscopy. Despite increased SsrB expression, activation of the SsrB-dependent reporter P sifA was reduced, indicating serovar-specific differences in SsrB activity. Unexpectedly, deletion of ssrB in S . Typhi increased net intracellular replication and host cell cytotoxicity, contrasting with its essential role in S . Typhimurium. Using phosphorylation incompetent (D56A) and acid-sensing deficient (H12Q) mutants, we demonstrate that the reduction in cytotoxicity is phosphorylation-independent, whereas restriction of net intracellular replication requires both phosphorylation and acid-dependent DNA binding. The SPI-2 effector SifB partially mediates this restriction. Our findings show that SsrB contributes to two infection phenotypes with distinct genetic requirements: it limits host cytotoxicity in a manner that requires neither phosphorylation nor acid-enhanced DNA binding, and it restricts intracellular replication in a manner that requires both activities. Whether these two phenotypes are executed by separate or by shared downstream targets was not established here. These results challenge the assumption that SsrB is dispensable for S . Typhi pathogenesis and highlight serovar-specific virulence strategies.

**Abstract**

Endogenous electric field (EF) plays a crucial role in the wound healing process, yet conventional electrical stimulation strategies remain constrained by their dependence on bulky external power supplies. Herein, we report a bilayer electrogenerative smart dressing (B-ESD) that generates a self-sustained EF through a robust streaming potential induced by unidirectional pumping of wound exudate. This biofluid-driven mechanism successfully amplified the attenuated wound potential from 343.8 to 921.0 mV without requiring any external power source. Comprehensive in vitro and in vivo evaluations demonstrated that this self-sustained EF mitigated acute inflammation, accelerated fibroblast migration, and promoted highly organized collagen deposition. Immunohistochemical analysis further revealed that the B-ESD regulated the expression of Type I collagen, CD31, and transforming growth factor-beta 1 at the wound site, improving the quality of wound repair. Consequently, the B-ESD achieves a 98.72% wound closure rate within 14 days, with reduced scar formation and the regrowth of mature skin appendages. Moreover, the B-ESD serves as a high-fidelity sensor to monitor physiological motions, offering a promising integrated platform that combines bioelectric wound therapy with real-time biomechanical monitoring for advanced wound management in the future.

### 339. [Ferroelectric Domain-Wall Motion, Stability, and Frequency Responses under Different Screening-Charge Conservation Laws](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/a7a0add5ddaefbfd7b5d9b1d516b6caab96bf460)

**Authors:** Sergei V. Kalinin  
**Venue/category:** cond-mat.mtrl-sci  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**arXiv:** `2610.13619`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar, arXiv  
**Keyword score:** 9  
**Matched:** mixed ionic-electronic conductor  

**Abstract**

We analyze ferroelectric domain wall motion, shape, and internal structure arising from coupling between polarization and screening charge. Universal cases are organized by electrical boundary conditions, charge conservation and dynamics, and polarization free energy. We consider exposed surfaces, interfaces beneath a dielectric layer and top electrode, and interfaces with a blocking mixed ionic-electronic conductor. Screening occurs through local exchange, exchange with lateral transport, or strictly conserved redistribution. Analytical derivations and finite-depth calculations establish screening drag, velocity branches, and conditions for spatial instability. For an isolated wall joining differently compensated domains, strict surface conservation and zero far-field current exclude steady translation of the screening profile, while allowing transient displacement, finite-sample arrest, and charge-balanced structural changes. Conservation also constrains long-distance transport around a moving junction in an adjoining conductor. Large-field simulations in the top-electrode geometry reveal nonlinear corrugation, transfer toward longer wavelengths, and coupled polarization rotation. First-order polarization energetics allow two interphase fronts enclosing a paraelectric core; multiaxial polarization permits wall rotation and competing intermediate ferroelectric domains. A driven structural-screening reduction supports stable periodic behavior. Reduced-variable diagrams summarize motion and structural regimes, while BaTiO3 and BiFeO3 examples connect them to material thermodynamics. Small-signal analysis distinguishes displacement, polarization, and electrostatic response functions. These results provide a framework for wall dynamics and structural transitions under different driving and screening conditions.

### 340. [Highly Luminescent Alkyl- and Alkoxy-Terminated Silicon Quantum Dots via Aging Following Single-Step Plasma Synthesis and Functionalization](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/d03802f5f6046ad913265687ed638a3f447f8da4)

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Nonthermal plasma synthesis enables the continuous production and immediate surface functionalization of silicon quantum dots (Si QDs) in a single, flow-through reactor. While this process occurs on a millisecond time scale, the resulting nanocrystals are often limited by low photoluminescence quantum yields (PLQY) due to persistent surface defects. In this work, we demonstrate that a minimal post-treatment, aging the Si QDs in specific solvents, can enhance the PLQY to ∼50%, approaching the performance of much more complex solution-phase methods. We find that the choice of the surface ligand and solvent is critical: hexanol-terminated Si QDs exhibit significantly higher initial and final PLQYs than decene-terminated particles, likely due to the faster reaction of alcohols with electrophilic surface sites in the gas phase. Among the solvents investigated, chloroform consistently yields the highest PLQY. Furthermore, we show that light irradiation at 400 and 633 nm drastically accelerates the Si QD aging process, reducing the aging time from hundreds of hours to under 20 h. This practical “synthesize and forget” strategy offers an efficient pathway for optimizing high-PLQY Si QDs for applications in optoelectronics and bioimaging.

### 341. [Tuning Interfacial Electron Transfer for Boosting Electrochemiluminescence: Methods, Mechanisms, and Bioanalytical Applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/b2d94eff0e5b8c18b7bb6482b907fd86e9e6013f)

**Authors:** Jing-Shuai Li, Hai-Fei Wan, Wen-Ling Gu, Liu-Yong Hu, Cheng-Zhou Zhu

**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.chemrev.6c00330`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

The interfacial electron-transfer process acts as a pivotal kinetic determinant of electrochemiluminescence (ECL) efficiency, governing the generation of excited-state emitters and thus dictating the intensity and stability of ECL signals. Currently, harnessing diverse nanomaterials has driven significant advances in the development of novel ECL luminophores and the activation of coreactants. However, most of these luminophores exhibit low ECL efficiency in aqueous media, and the intrinsic reaction and activation mechanisms remain ambiguous because of their complex structures and compositions. Notably, tuning electron-transfer pathways at the molecular/atomic level can not only enable efficient signal amplification but also facilitate the tracking of reaction pathways, thereby providing deeper insights into the fundamental mechanisms underlying ECL processes. This Review comprehensively summarizes advances during the past five years in tuning interfacial electron transfer to enhance ECL. Furthermore, we highlight progress in electron-transfer-mediated signal amplification strategies for highly sensitive ECL biosensing and bioimaging applications. Finally, we propose and discuss the challenges and perspectives associated with improving the ECL efficiency through the modulation of electron transfer processes. Precise regulation of interfacial electron transfer holds substantial promise for advancing ECL theories, clarifying reaction mechanisms, and expanding the scope of ECL-based bioanalytical applications.

**Abstract**

Organic photodetectors (OPDs) hold significant promise for applications spanning medical monitoring, bioimaging and night vision due to their unique advantages, such as flexibility, light weight, and potential for large-area fabrication. Nevertheless, the dark current density (Jd) of OPDs exhibits a pronounced increase under applied reverse bias, which consequently degrades the specific detectivity (D*). To tackle this challenge, this work proposes an interface engineering strategy based on Sn/Cl co-doped ZnO (SCZO). The doping effectively reduces oxygen defects in ZnO, causing the valence band maximum (VBM) to shift downward, thereby suppressing hole injection under bias and reducing Jd. Experiments show that the SCZO-based device exhibits a Jd of 4.05 × 10-11 A cm-2 at -0.1 V, nearly one order of magnitude lower than that of the control device (4.07 × 10-10 A cm-2). Notably, the SCZO-based OPD enables real-time and accurate monitoring of human heart rate, demonstrating its considerable potential for health-monitoring applications. Furthermore, OPDs incorporating the SCZO interface exhibit excellent imaging performance under 820 nm illumination. These findings underscore the significant potential of SCZO-based OPDs for flexible, wearable health monitoring and near-infrared imaging applications.

### 343. [Remote Control of Excited-State Chemistry in Positive Reversibly Switchable Fluorescent Proteins](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/7429861fe359305a89be668d54acb6a078a139a6)
