**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Positive reversibly switchable fluorescent proteins (+rsFPs) are genetically encoded fluorescent labels allowing super-resolution microscopy without the fading inherent in the better established negative (−) rsFPs. Typically, switching is initiated by an ultrafast excited-state isomerization reaction. Here, we investigate the ultrafast photophysics of four structurally similar variants of the archetypal +rsFP Padron, including Padron0.9, Padron2, Kohinoor, and a newly reported “reverse” Kohinoor mutant (Kohinoor S176F) using ultrafast optical and vibrational spectroscopy. Padron0.9 exhibits an efficient excited-state proton transfer (ESPT) reaction in parallel with photoisomerization, whereas Kohinoor and Padron2 do not. Remarkably, introducing the single reverse mutation S176F in Kohinoor restores Padron-like ESPT dynamics, even though the mutation is remote from the proton donor. This result demonstrates that a single amino acid residue near to, but not in direct H-bonded interaction with, the chromophore can govern the fate of excited states. TRIR measurements reveal distinct changes in the local hydrogen-bonding network and chromophore–protein interactions associated with ESPT-active variants and further reveal a key role for H-bonding between the chromophore and an arginine residue through a study of the R66K mutant of Padron0.9. Overall, these results show that subtle mutations shift the excited state between proton transfer and photoisomerization pathways, providing mechanistic insight into photoswitching in rsFPs, thus guiding the design of improved fluorescent proteins for superresolution bioimaging.

### 344. [Nanomedicines based on renewable materials for sustainable drug delivery and biomedical imaging applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/ff9c35fc8d336c2d732af459a352345ff774f1fd)

**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

With the application of nanoscale materials, nanomedicine is redefining and improving the delivery of pharmaceuticals and molecular bioimaging probes, providing novel approaches to disease diagnosis, management, and treatment. As in many other fields, renewable materials are becoming increasingly important in nanomedicine because they offer sustainable alternatives to fossil-based resources and are often biodegradable and biocompatible. Additionally, these materials are replenishable in nature, and some can be derived or recovered from industrial and agro-industrial waste streams. In this review, we critically evaluate nanomedicines fabricated from renewable polymers, polysaccharides, proteins, and lipids for drug delivery and biomedical imaging, together with their physicochemical and biological characterization. Particular emphasis is placed on how material origin and physicochemical properties influence formulation performance and clinical readiness. Rather than considering renewable origin alone as an indicator of translational suitability, the major material classes are compared in terms of source consistency, biodegradability, imaging functionality, and regulatory maturity. Recent developments reported during 2020–2026 are discussed, with particular attention to the objectives and key findings of the developed nanomedicines. Additionally, their applications in the treatment of various diseases and in biomedical imaging are reviewed. The review also highlights key translational barriers, and evidence gaps that must be addressed to advance renewable material-based nanomedicines from proof-of-concept systems toward reproducible, scalable, and clinically relevant products.

Achieving triple-fluorescence from a pure organic compound in solution is nearly impossible, as Kasha's rule only allows single fluorescence from the lowest excited state. This work provides a new strategy to activate triple-fluorescence from a single-component organic compound, opening a new avenue toward multichannel photonic materials and NIR‑II bioimaging.

DTBTC enables confocal cellular imaging at an exceptionally low concentration of 500 nM and efficiently generates reactive oxygen species under light irradiation, producing potent photocytotoxicity with IC50 values of 332 and 646 nM against HeLa and MCF-7 monolayer cells, respectively. It also demonstrates significant therapeutic efficacy in the corresponding tumors pheroid models (IC50 < 20 μM). Compared with reference compounds R1 [7-(diethylamino)-2-oxo-2H-chromene-3-carboxylic acid] and R2 [coumarin-3-carboxylic acid], DTBTC displays distinctive aggregation-dependent photophysical behavior. In solution, it exhibits bright green emission with a high photoluminescence quantum yield (81.14%), a narrow emission bandwidth (FWHM = 41 nm), and a small Stokes shift (39 nm), characteristic of monomeric emission. Upon aggregation, the appearance of a dominant blueshifted absorption band (449 nm) and a weaker redshifted band (491 nm) indicates the coexistence of H- and T-type aggregates. While H-aggregates promote emission quenching, T-aggregates give rise to orange-red emission. This aggregation-sensitive luminescence further enables fluorescence turn-off detection of acids (acid detection threshold corresponds to an estimated pH of ~6.01) in water, with high sensitivity even toward weak acids such as acetic acid.

### 347. [From Photochromism to Nanoscopy: The Emerging Role of Fluorescent Diarylethene Photoswitches in Super‐Resolution Imaging](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/224b182581fb13a30b0d494c72d716c0241ec34c)

**Authors:** Yu-Zhu Zheng, Lin-Yu Zhang, Yang-Yang Wang, Ya-Qin Yang, Zi-Yong Li, Pei Zhang, Hui Guo, Jun Yin  
**Venue/category:** Laser & Photonics Reviews  
**Date:** 2026-10-18  
**DOI:** `10.1002/lpor.72072`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Super‐resolution imaging (SRI) achieves nanoscale biological visualization by precisely controlling fluorophore emission over time, a strategy known as “trading time for space”. Fluorescent diarylethene (DAE) photoswitches, with exceptional reversibility, thermal stability, fatigue resistance, and tunable photochromism, enable this approach by reversible switching between fluorescent “on” and dark “off” states, allowing neighboring molecules within the diffraction limit to be distinguished. Fluorescent DAE derivatives have recently garnered increasing attention as fluorescent labels for SRI applications, offering decisive advantages over conventional photoswitches. Despite considerable progress, a systematic review specifically addressing the application of DAEs in SRI remains absent. Herein, we present a comprehensive survey of fluorescent DAE photoswitches for SRI, centering on molecular design strategies and recent advances. We categorize these systems into four classes based on their activation modes and driving mechanisms: “turn‐on” fluorescent DAEs, “turn‐off” fluorescent DAEs, polymeric fluorescent DAEs, and visible‐light‐driven DAEs. For each category, we highlight representative structures, key design rationales, and imaging performance benchmarks. We conclude by identifying current challenges and outlining future perspectives, aiming to guide the rational development of next‐generation fluorescent DAE photoswitches for advanced super‐resolution bioimaging.

### 348. [Recent Advances in 1,8-Naphthalimide-Based Supramolecular Gels (2020–2026)](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/4362d279f16a4b5996a6651f541bd35f1cbf8c0a)

**Authors:** Abelardo Sánchez Oliva, Iván Grande Morales, Eva Luna Golvano, Iván Torres-Moya  
**Venue/category:** Gels  
**Date:** 2026-10-16  
**DOI:** `10.3390/gels12100933`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Naphthalimide (NI) derivatives constitute attractive building blocks for the construction of fluorescent supramolecular gels owing to their structural versatility, remarkable photophysical properties, and ability to participate in non-covalent interactions. These properties prompted us to write this review, that provides a focused overview of the representative advances reported between 2020 and 2026 in NI-based supramolecular gels, covering the evolution from low-molecular-weight organogelators and metallogels to peptide-based and water-compatible hydrogels. Particular attention is paid to the influence of molecular design on self-assembly and gelation, including the contribution of hydrogen bonding, π–π stacking, metal coordination, and other intermolecular interactions to the formation of fibrillar supramolecular networks. The close relationship between aggregation and the photophysical response of the NI chromophore is also discussed, highlighting phenomena such as aggregation-induced and aggregation-enhanced emission and gelation-induced fluorescence enhancement. These properties have enabled the development of stimuli-responsive materials displaying fluorescence modulation and gel–sol or gel–gel transitions, particularly in response to metal ions and chemical stimuli. Recent studies also further demonstrate a transition toward more complex functional systems, including fluorescent peptide hydrogels and thixotropic materials with potential biomedical applications. The main applications developed during this period, ranging from chemical sensing to smart fluorescent materials, bioimaging, and biomedical systems, are critically analyzed. Finally, current limitations and future opportunities for NI-based gels are discussed, with particular emphasis on the development of red and near-infrared emissive systems and advanced soft photonic materials.

---

### 349. [Green fluorescent carbon dots derived from cotton stalks for in situ labeling of cotton fibers](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/9deff9f611388118d3c23eddf4de46fa3ed1652c)

**Authors:** Hui Sun, Rui Wang, Juan Wu, Ying Ni, Ling-Zhong Wan, S. Ye, Huan-Yong Han, Di Chen, Xiao-Nan Deng, Jia-Bao Zhu  
**Venue/category:** BMC Plant Biology  
**Date:** 2026-10-16  
**DOI:** `10.1186/s12870-026-10211-7`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

In-situ fluorescent labeling of cotton fibers using biomass-derived carbon dots remains underexplored. Conventional labeling techniques may damage fiber structure, exhibit limited environmental stability, and incur relatively high costs, restricting their practical application in sustainable cotton traceability. Carbon dots possess favorable photoluminescence properties, good biocompatibility, and low toxicity, showing promising potential for plant bioimaging and in-situ tracking. This provides a feasible route for developing low-damage, cost-effective, and stable labeling approaches for cotton fibers. In this study, green fluorescent carbon dots (G-CDs) were hydrothermally synthesized using renewable cotton stalk waste as the carbon source, and their applicability for in-situ fluorescent labeling of cotton fibers was investigated. The prepared G-CDs exhibited excitation-dependent photoluminescence, with optimal excitation at 410 nm, emission at 530 nm, and a quantum yield of 5.99%. TEM characterization revealed uniformly dispersed G-CDs nanoparticles with particle sizes ranging from 1.75 to 4.25 nm and an average diameter of 2.67 nm. FTIR and XPS analyses confirmed abundant hydrophilic functional groups (–OH, C = O, and C–N) on the G-CDs surface. G-CDs were supplied to cotton plants via root irrigation from the seedling stage to the boll-opening stage. CLSM observation and quantitative fluorescence analysis showed that G-CDs were absorbed by cotton roots (56.281 ± 1.073), translocated upward to stems (52.413 ± 2.003) and leaves (41.306 ± 2.063), and eventually accumulated in mature cotton fibers (29.985 ± 0.939). Furthermore, G-CDs labeling did not induce significant changes in cotton growth or fiber quality. All growth-related indicators and fiber quality parameters showed no statistically significant differences between the control and treatment groups ( p > 0.05). G-CDs achieved in-situ fluorescent labeling of cotton fibers without impairing plant growth or fiber quality, while the synthesis strategy enabled the reuse of agricultural cotton stalk waste. It is suggested that this dual advantage facilitates non-destructive cotton labeling and the sustainable utilization of biomass waste. This work illustrates the applicable potential of biomass-derived carbon dots for in planta fiber labeling and cotton traceability research.

### 353. [Regioisomeric engineering of carbazole–naphthalimide donor–acceptor systems enables efficient triplet generation and room temperature phosphorescence in molecular and nanoaggregated states](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/109e1edca39b93f4d098676728ba5c61a71dafa9)

**Authors:** Pietro Mancini, Siddan Gouthaman, Vignesh Rajendran, Chiara Montanari, V. Titone, K. R. Justin Thomas, B. Carlotti  
**Venue/category:** Chemical Science  
**Date:** 2026-10-14  
**DOI:** `10.1039/d6sc06161c`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Regioisomeric engineering provides an effective strategy for controlling excited-state dynamics in donor–acceptor systems, yet its influence on triplet generation and room-temperature phosphorescence (RTP) remains insufficiently understood. Herein, we report two series of carbazole–phenyl–naphthalimide donor–acceptor regioisomers in which carbazole is connected to either the C3 or C4 position of the naphthalimide acceptor through ortho-, meta-, and para-substituted phenyl linkers. Systematic photophysical investigations reveal that subtle changes in molecular connectivity profoundly influence charge-transfer character, fluorescence and intersystem crossing efficiency. Para-linked and C4-substituted derivatives exhibit enhanced fluorescence owing to improved conjugation and reduced structural relaxation, whereas ortho- and meta-isomers as well as C3-substitution promote more efficient triplet generation through spin–orbit charge-transfer-assisted intersystem crossing, yielding triplet quantum yields approaching unity. Femtosecond and nanosecond transient absorption spectroscopy directly elucidate the singlet-to-triplet conversion dynamics, while TD-DFT calculations and single-crystal X-ray diffraction establish clear relationships between molecular geometry, electronic coupling, and excited-state behaviour. Efficient orange-to-red RTP is observed in benzophenone host–guest matrices as well as in water-dispersed nanoaggregates, suggesting that regioisomeric control can activate persistent phosphorescence in both solid-state and colloidal environments. These findings provide valuable molecular design principles for developing purely organic RTP materials for optoelectronics, time-resolved bioimaging, and photonic applications.

### 354. [Engineering reduced graphene oxide hybrid nanocomposites for biomedicine: progress, challenges and emerging opportunities](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/7bb14fd854211d84bb87f684d7964853d0bdc3b6)

**Venue/category:** Polymer international  
**Date:** 2026-10-14  
**DOI:** `10.1002/pi.70206`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Hybrid nanocomposites based on reduced graphene oxide (rGO) have emerged as a versatile class of multifunctional materials for advanced biomedical applications owing to their exceptional physicochemical properties, tunable surface chemistry, high surface area and improved biocompatibility. This review critically examines recent advances in the design, synthesis, functionalization and biomedical applications of rGO hybrid nanocomposites, emphasizing synergistic integration with polymers, metals, metal oxides, ceramics and biomolecules. The review discusses their applications in drug and gene delivery, cancer therapy, antimicrobial systems, tissue engineering, regenerative medicine, biosensing and bioimaging in detail. rGO hybrids exhibit high drug‐loading capacity, stimuli‐responsive release, enhanced cellular uptake, synergistic chemo‐photothermal effects, broad‐spectrum antibacterial activity, improved mechanical reinforcement and highly sensitive biosensing for real‐time diagnostics. Despite these advances, clinical translation remains constrained by concerns about long‐term toxicity, fabrication scalability and reproducibility, regulatory approval, and the lack of standardized biological evaluation protocols. Emerging trends, including stimuli‐responsive nanoplatforms, artificial‐intelligence‐assisted materials design, personalized theranostics, and sustainable manufacturing strategies, are also highlighted. Finally, the review identifies key research priorities to bridge the gap between laboratory innovation and clinical implementation, underscoring the significant potential of rGO hybrid nanocomposites for next‐generation precision medicine and healthcare technologies. © 2026 Society of Chemical Industry.

### 355. [Dual-Responsive Dextran-Based Nanosystem for NIR Dual-Modal Imaging-Guided Photothermal-Chemotherapy of Breast Cancer](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/e35e84f6fb7f621ea0b6d3380d453320b4c26887)

**Abstract**

Breast cancer is a highly prevalent malignancy, but the conventional single chemotherapy suffers from insufficient therapeutic outcomes. To overcome this challenge, this study developed a dextran-based prodrug nanogel (DDIR) with dual pH/GSH responsiveness to the tumor microenvironment, enabling near-infrared (NIR) fluorescence−photothermal dual-modal imaging-guided chemo-photothermal synergistic therapy. Specifically, DDIR was prepared by conjugating doxorubicin to aldehyde-functionalized dextran via acid-sensitive Schiff-base linkages, crosslinking with a disulfide-containing linker, and encapsulating the NIR-II photothermal agent IR-1061 through hydrophobic interactions. In the acidic tumor microenvironment with elevated glutathione (GSH) levels, DDIR release drugs of DOX and IR-1061, and efficiently suppresses tumor proliferation, and ablates tumor lesions in tumor-bearing mice. This study provides a novel theranostic platform for the synergistic treatment of breast cancer and holds a promising potential for clinical translation.

### 356. [Cu2+ coordinated L-proline-derived carbon-nanodots for specific non-enzymatic detection of tyrosine.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/966031741cdd2069609a4456015b69d8b7fb1a6a)

**Abstract**

Carbon-nanodots (CDs) exhibit versatile emissive and electrochemical properties, which have been widely utilized in applications such as biosensing, bioimaging, and light-emitting devices. Amino acids provide an attractive means for introducing nitrogen (N-doping) and surface functionalities with structural diversity into CDs. In this work, water-dispersible, emissive and electrochemically active L-proline derived CDs (CDs-Pro) were synthesized directly from L-proline through a mild thermal process. The supramolecular complex of the CDs-Pro with Cu2+ ions (CDs-Pro/Cu2+) was evaluated for luminescence and electrochemical non-enzymatic detection of tyrosine (Tyr). The CDs-Pro and CDs-Pro/Cu2+ nanostructures were systematically characterized using various analytical methods to confirm the emissive core structure as well as the outer-shell functionality. The optical investigations report an interesting pH-dependent photoluminescence quantum yield in the range of 1.2 to 2.2%. The surfaces of screen-printed carbon electrodes modified with the CDs-Pro/Cu2+ nanostructures showed specific non-enzymatic electrochemical recognition of Tyr over a linear range of 16-550 µM, with a limit of detection (LOD) of 61.8 µM. Meanwhile, the water dispersion of CDs-Pro/Cu2+ nanostructures exhibited specific non-enzymatic photoluminescence detection of Tyr over the range 5-350 µM, with an LOD value of approximately 0.41 µM. Moreover, assay specificity was investigated using molecular modelling methods. The formation free energy (ΔGform) values obtained for all investigated aromatic amino acids confirm a higher stability for the Pro/Cu2+/Tyr2- system (-661.86 kJ mol-1) compared to Pro/Cu2+/Trp (-640.60 kJ mol-1) and Pro/Cu2+/Phe (-637.98 kJ mol-1). Finally, this article addresses current challenges in the field of carbon-based nanostructures and outlines future research directions, emphasizing the potential of carbon-nanodots for the development of non-enzymatic assays for amino acid recognition.

A new heteroleptic luminescent and photocytotoxic ruthenium(II) polypyridyl complex, [RuII(bphen)2(cur)]+ (bphen = bathophenanthroline, cur = curcumin), has been thoroughly investigated for its photophysical properties with potential as a luminescent probe for bioimaging and a photocytotoxic agent. The optimized lead complex was obtained via coordinating curcumin to the Ru(II) center pre-coordinated with two bathophenanthroline ligands, producing a stable cationic structure with extended π-conjugation. The incorporation of curcumin to {Ru(bphen)2} moiety improves emissive characteristics and also enhances the biological compatibility, owing to its natural origin and functional groups that interact with biomolecular environments. The complex exhibits good photostability and strong emission under physiological conditions, enabling its application as a luminescent stain for fluorescence-based cellular imaging. Furthermore, in vitro photocytotoxicity studies in the human breast cancer cell line MCF-7 reveal a significant photocytotoxicity index (PI ∼ 19.5), indicating low dark toxicity but an enhanced cytotoxic response upon visible-light irradiation. These findings highlight the dual functionality of the [RuII(bphen)2(cur)]+ as a theragnostic agent, serving as a luminescent bioimaging probe and a potential photosensitizing agent for light-activated therapeutic applications.

### 358. [g-C₃N₄-based smart nanocatalysts for photocatalytic cancer therapy: mechanisms, surface engineering, and therapeutic applications](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/81e23767956284b70718ef9c2e9589e4c493e36c)

**Authors:** A. Majedi  
**Venue/category:** Health Nanotechnology  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.1186/s44301-026-00041-9`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Cancer remains one of the leading causes of mortality worldwide, necessitating the development of more precise and effective therapeutic strategies. Graphitic carbon nitride (g-C₃N₄) has emerged as a highly promising metal-free semiconductor nanomaterial for photocatalytic cancer therapy owing to its tunable bandgap (~2.7 eV), favorable biocompatibility, chemical stability and large surface area. Recent advances in nanotechnology and surface engineering have transformed g-C₃N₄ from a conventional photocatalyst into a multifunctional nanotheranostic platform for multimodal cancer treatment. This review provides a comprehensive and up-to-date overview of g-C₃N₄-based nanocatalysts, covering photocatalytic mechanisms, engineering strategies, therapeutic applications, biosafety and translational prospects. Under light irradiation, g-C₃N₄ generates reactive oxygen species (ROS) through photoinduced electron–hole separation, triggering oxidative stress-mediated cancer cell death through multiple regulated and non-regulated cell-death pathways, including apoptosis, ferroptosis, necrosis, and, in specific engineered systems, cuproptosis. Advanced engineering strategies including heteroatom doping, heterojunction construction, oxygen vacancy engineering, noble metal decoration and biomimetic surface functionalization have been employed to improve charge separation, extend light responsiveness toward the NIR region, enhance ROS generation, and facilitate tumor targeting. This review highlights recent progress in multimodal therapeutic approaches integrating photodynamic therapy (PDT), photothermal therapy (PTT), chemodynamic therapy (CDT), drug delivery, gas therapy, immunotherapy and nanozyme-mediated catalytic therapy. Emerging g-C₃N₄-based nanozymes with multi-enzyme activities (OXD, POD, CAT, GSHOx, and SOD), together with bioimaging-guided theranostic systems, further expand the therapeutic potential of these nanoplatforms. Finally, key challenges including limited light penetration, hypoxic tumor microenvironment, elevated glutathione levels, biosafety concerns and clinical translation barriers are critically discussed. This review highlights the substantial potential of g-C₃N₄-based nanoplatforms for next-generation precision oncology and clinical translation.

---

### 359. [Catalyst-free Polyannulations of Potassium Sulfide and Unactivated Alkynes toward Multifunctional Thiophene-Containing Polymers](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/cb5f2e92003340808dc19bba7ba88d30f5b81b1f)

**Abstract**

Facile and efficient transformation of inexpensive inorganic sulfides into high-value polymer materials is of great academic and industrial significance. Herein, we report a catalyst-free polyannulation strategy between potassium sulfide and internal diynes, which can efficiently produce thiophene-containing polymers (TCPs) in N,N-dimethylformamide under mild conditions. The reaction may proceed via a trisulfur radical anion-mediated mechanism, generating thiophene units in situ without the need for thiophene-containing monomers, additives, or transition metals. The resulting TCPs exhibit high molecular weights (up to 16,300 g/mol), excellent solubility and film-forming ability, and remarkable thermal stability. Their thin films display high refractive indices (n589.2 = 1.6579–1.8078) and UV-tunable optical properties. Post-oxidation transforms the polymers into aggregation-induced emission-active materials with remarkably enhanced fluorescence quantum yields in aggregate and solid states. The aggregate-state luminescent TCPs and their S,S-dioxide derivatives demonstrate organelle-specific imaging capabilities and excellent biocompatibility, highlighting their potential for advanced optical and bioimaging applications.

### 360. [Carbon dot-based nanomaterials in neurodegeneration: addressing neurobiological barriers and targeting challenges.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/dbca4292b05d1d2f1fd919a41ae1b0af260bd17e)

**Authors:** B. Unnikrishnan, A. Anand, Chih-Nan Fang, Shou-Mei Wu, Chun-Chi Wang, Chih-Ching Huang, Han-Wei Chu, Huan-Tsung Chang  
**Venue/category:** Chemical Communications  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.1039/d6cc03262a`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Neurodegenerative diseases, including Alzheimer's disease, Parkinson's disease, Huntington's disease, and amyotrophic lateral sclerosis, represent a major global health challenge due to their progressive nature and limited therapeutic options. One of the primary obstacles to treating these disorders is the blood-brain barrier (BBB), which restricts the delivery of most therapeutic agents into the central nervous system. In recent years, carbon-based nanomaterials, particularly carbon dot (CD) nanostructures, have emerged as promising platforms for neurological applications owing to their ultrasmall size, BBB-crossing ability, and anti-inflammatory and neuroprotective properties, in addition to tunable surface chemistry, strong photoluminescence, and favorable biocompatibility. This review provides a comprehensive overview of recent research developments in the application of CD-based nanomaterials, including carbon nanodots, carbon quantum dots, graphene quantum dots, and carbon polymer dots, for the potential treatment of neurodegenerative diseases. The synthesis strategies and carbonization approaches used to produce these carbon nanomaterials are discussed, along with their physicochemical properties relevant to neurobiological applications, such as particle size, surface functionalization, heteroatom doping, optical properties, and stability in biological environments. Furthermore, the mechanisms by which CD-based nanomaterials can traverse the BBB are examined, including receptor-mediated and adsorptive-mediated transcytosis pathways. In addition, their applications are discussed, with particular focus on their roles in bioimaging, biosensing, inhibition of pathological protein aggregation, antioxidative activity, and targeted drug delivery. Finally, key challenges related to toxicity, biodistribution, large-scale synthesis, and clinical translation are discussed, along with future perspectives for the development of multifunctional theranostic platforms. Overall, use of CD-based nanomaterials represents a promising emerging strategy for overcoming neurobiological barriers and enabling the development of next-generation nanotherapeutics for the management of neurodegenerative diseases.

---

### 361. [Continuously Tunable Localized Surface Plasmon Resonance of Anisotropic Gold Nanopolyhedrons for Multiplexed Surface-Enhanced Raman Scattering Imaging.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/0cf11b240d22ec220d7906be052bdc68a454437f)

**Abstract**

Surface-enhanced Raman scattering (SERS) is attractive for multiplex bioimaging owing to its excellent photostability and ultranarrow spectral bandwidth. However, achieving synergistic enhancement of excitation and Raman scattering fields remains challenging. Herein, the relationship between geometry and localized surface plasmon resonance (LSPR) was investigated by continuously tuning the aspect ratio (AR) of anisotropic Au nanopolyhedrons (Au NPHs). Central-body modulation while preserving sharp terminal tips enables continuous LSPR tuning while retaining tip-associated electromagnetic enhancement. The SERS behavior was further analyzed by considering the spectral matching between the LSPR and the excitation/scattering wavelengths. For different excitation wavelengths, the optimal SERS response can be achieved by selecting Au NPHs whose LSPR band best matches with the excitation and Raman scattering wavelengths. These results demonstrate that Raman enhancement is governed by spectral matching rather than a fixed optimal geometry. The optimized Au NPHs enabled the use of dual SERS probes for proof-of-concept multiplex biomarker imaging.

**Abstract**

Boron-containing compounds have received special interest over the last few years owing to their favorable photophysical characteristics, stimulating their use as fluorescent sensors, in bioimaging and mechanofluorochromic materials. However, their synthesis is often time-consuming and requires specialized protocols. Here, we report the design and synthesis of a novel class of monofluoroborate compounds featuring π-expanded coumarin subunits, with simple access in relatively high yields, and investigate, by computational and experimental approaches, their photophysical properties in solution and in the solid state. These compounds exhibit intense absorption in the 350-450 nm range and show fluorescence in the solid state, while are not luminescent in solution. The absence of emission in fluid solution is ascribed to ultrafast radiationless decay via conical intersection, as suggested by computational data. Ultrafast pump-probe transient absorption spectroscopy in fluid solution confirms the excited-state computed data, indicating that the excited state decays within few picoseconds to the ground state. The structural rearrangement needed for reaching the conical intersection is inhibited in the solid state thanks to the presence of dimers with restricted geometrical flexibility, as evidenced by crystallographic studies and calculations, giving rise to solid-state emission.

### 363. [Coronavirus spherules are supported by a novel triple-layer protein lattice](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/c6bf02f0033af897bc175c07004cfaae5f4ab4e9)

**Authors:** Patrick M. Phillips, Joanna Wells, Naveen Mathew, Nicole Doyle, S. Keep, P. Majumder, J. B. Gilchrist, Philippa C. Hawes, Jonathan M. Grimes, Maria Harkiolaki, D. Clare, Helena J. Maier  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.09.757900`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 8  
**Matched:** bioimaging  

**Abstract**

Coronaviruses (CoVs) cause significant disease in humans and animals, with a track-record of cross-species transmission. Despite their global significance, available control strategies are limited, especially for emerging CoVs. A deeper fundamental understanding of the virus life cycle is essential to underpin development of control measures. A critical step of the CoV life cycle is the rearrangement of host cell membranes into replication organelles (ROs). This is conserved across all CoVs and ROs comprise double membrane vesicles (DMVs), the site of viral RNA synthesis, and double membrane spherules. CoV spherules are complex structures requiring both positive and negative curvature of paired membranes so are predicted to have a high energy cost to maintain. To date they have an entirely unknown function. Interestingly, although always present, abundance of spherules in CoV infected cells varies with Betacoronavirus Middle East Respiratory Syndrome coronavirus, Gammacoronavirus infectious bronchitis virus (IBV) and porcine deltacoronavirus in particular producing a considerable number. Here, using cryo-bioimaging we identified for the first time that CoV spherules are embedded with a complex and organised triple-layered protein lattice and that spherules always contain a cargo that appears arranged as a shell inside the vesicle. In addition, we confirmed the presence of a double membrane spanning molecular pore in Gammacoronavirus DMVs and further identified a population of potential DMV formation intermediates. Together, this work provides a foundation for understanding a fundamental yet unresolved aspect of coronavirus replication.

---

Second harmonic generation (SHG) microscopy is a nonlinear optical technique with intrinsic sensitivity to molecular ordering at interfaces, making it well suited for probing small-molecule interactions with biological membranes. When combined with two-photon fluorescence (TPF) microscopy, complementary information on probe population and distribution can be obtained. While SHG microscopy has been widely applied to material and eukaryotic systems, its application to prokaryotic systems such as bacteria remains comparatively unexplored. Here, we employ simultaneous SHG/TPF microscopy to investigate interfacial heterogeneity in single bacterial cells. Using Staphylococcus aureus and Enterococcus faecalis as model systems, we obtain high-resolution SHG and TPF images that reveal distinct populations of small molecules interacting with the bacterial membrane, each giving rise to species-specific nonlinear responses.

### 381. [Lipid Nanoparticles–Protein Interactions: Biochemical and Biophysical Tools to Unveil the Protein Corona Biointerface and Drive Precision Delivery](https://doi.org/10.3390/biophysica6060104)

**Authors:** Ping Zhang, Jiang Shao, Ye Liang, Yao Li, Wenjuan Zhang  
**Venue/category:** Biophysica  
**Date:** 2026-10-22  
**DOI:** `10.3390/biophysica6060104`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** biointerface  

**Abstract**

Lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) represent the most clinically advanced non-viral nucleic acid delivery systems; yet, the protein corona (PC) that spontaneously assembles on their surface in biological fluids largely dictates their biodistribution, rendering the LNP–PC biointerface a key determinant in achieving the more efficient and selective delivery to non-hepatic organs. Conventional endpoint analyses fail to resolve the kinetic and structural complexity of the LNP–PC biointerface. This review provides a systematic overview of the methods for studying this biointerface, covering the isolation of LNP–PC complexes, such as differential centrifugation (DC), density gradient centrifugation (DGC), magnetic separation, photoaffinity separation, antibody-based pulldowns, size exclusion chromatography (SEC), and asymmetric flow field-flow fractionation (AF4); the characterization of PC properties, such as dynamic light scattering (DLS), multi-angle light scattering (MALS), nanoparticle tracking analysis (NTA), electrophoretic light scattering (ELS), transmission electron microscopy (TEM), nuclear magnetic resonance spectroscopy (NMR), fluorescence correlation spectroscopy (FCS), liquid chromatography-mass spectrometry (LC-MS), circular dichroism (CD), and analytical ultracentrifugation (AUC); the real-time monitoring of binding kinetics using quartz crystal microbalance with dissipation (QCM-D), surface plasmon resonance (SPR), and biolayer interferometry (BLI); the characterization of binding affinity by isothermal titration calorimetry (ITC), microscale thermophoresis (MST), and spectral shift (SpS); and high-throughput screening methods for LNPs. Integrating these tools permits the discrimination of hard corona (HC) and soft corona (SC) populations, the quantitative determination of adsorption kinetics and binding affinity, and the correlation of PC compositional fingerprints with LNP tropism. This integrated workflow transforms the LNP–PC biointerface from an ill-defined biological layer into a programmable information interface, offering a methodological guide to accelerate the rational design of next-generation LNPs.

### 383. [Optimizing Indocyanine Green Fluorescence Cholangiography in Laparoscopic Cholecystectomy: A Systematic Review of Randomized Controlled Trials on Dose, Timing, Route, Efficacy, and Safety](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/3fa3f5455f22a4e8c06a396597545127c977d46d)

**Authors:** Dimitrios Tsavdaris, G. Geropoulos, Anastasios Gialouris, Alexandros Mekras, K. Psarras, D. Paramythiotis  
**Venue/category:** Medicina  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.3390/medicina62102022`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** near-infrared fluorescence  

**Abstract**

Background and Objectives: Near-infrared fluorescence cholangiography with indocyanine green (ICG) has been proposed as an adjunct for improving biliary anatomy recognition during laparoscopic cholecystectomy. However, uncertainty remains regarding its comparative efficacy, operative value, safety, and optimal route, dose, and timing. Materials and Methods: A systematic review was conducted to identify randomized comparative and dose-finding studies evaluating ICG fluorescence cholangiography during laparoscopic cholecystectomy. Outcomes included visualization of the cystic duct, common hepatic duct, common bile duct, biliary junctions, operative time, time to critical anatomical identification, conversion or bailout procedures, length of stay, bile duct injury, bile leak, and ICG-related adverse events. Clinical and methodological heterogeneity precluded meta-analysis; findings were synthesized descriptively. Results: Eighteen studies were included. ICG improved visualization of selected biliary structures compared with white-light imaging, particularly before dissection, whereas differences versus radiographic intraoperative cholangiography were less pronounced. Operative effects were inconsistent: several studies reported shorter cholangiography duration or earlier anatomical identification, without consistent reductions in total operative time or length of stay. Bile duct injuries and bile leaks were infrequent, but event numbers were insufficient to establish a protective effect or comparative safety. Selected dose comparisons suggested reduced hepatic background fluorescence with lower doses, although findings depended on administration timing and imaging platform. Interpretation was limited by subjective visualization assessment, risk of bias, and heterogeneous patient populations. Conclusions: ICG fluorescence cholangiography improves visualization of selected biliary structures, but improved visualization should not be equated with demonstrated clinical benefit. Evidence remains insufficient to establish reductions in bile duct injury, conversion, or postoperative morbidity, or a universally optimal route, dose, or timing regimen.

### 384. [Nanoscale Buried Interface Control in Silicone Hydrogels Enables a Biointerface Engineering Platform](https://doi.org/10.1002/advs.78148)

**Authors:** Po‐Hsiang Chen, Jia‐Hua Wang, Ching Chen, Shih‐Hsuan Fu, Rajan Deepan Chakravarthy, Hsin‐Chieh Lin  
**Venue/category:** Advanced Science  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.1002/advs.78148`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** biointerface  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT Engineering multifunctional biointerfaces requires quantitative understanding of how buried nanoscale coating architectures govern biological function, yet such relationships remain inaccessible because non‐destructive characterization of soft, curved interfaces is lacking. Here, we establish an integrated engineering–characterization–function platform that quantitatively resolves buried nanocoatings and directly correlates their architecture with interfacial performance. Using silicone hydrogel contact lenses as a representative biomedical substrate, robust semi‐interpenetrating polymer network (semi‐IPN) nanocoatings are fabricated and characterized by gas cluster ion beam X‐ray photoelectron spectroscopy (GCIB‐XPS), time‐of‐flight secondary ion mass spectrometry (ToF‐SIMS), and a newly developed atomic force microscopy (AFM) modulus‐based algorithm. This multimodal framework non‐destructively maps vertical chemical gradients, nanometer‐scale layer thicknesses, and interfacial mechanical distributions with sub‐100 nm precision, enabling quantitative visualization of conformal buried nanocoatings. The resulting structure–function relationships guide the rational engineering of poly(2‐hydroxyethyl methacrylate) (poly(HEMA)) interfaces with homogeneous surface coverage (∼89%), enhanced wettability, markedly reduced protein adsorption and cellular fouling, while fully preserving oxygen permeability, optical transparency, UV protection, and mechanical integrity. The platform further enables incorporation of functional monomers for light‐triggered therapeutic release without compromising device performance. Beyond contact lenses, this work provides a generalizable strategy for quantitatively engineering and predicting multifunctional nanocoatings across diverse biomedical interfaces.

### 385. [Functional assessment of experimental lymphedema: a focused mini-review of animal models and measurement methods](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/69220ea46a5240a7db1b89fbb23a9f2c15fc959d)

**Authors:** Shao-Yun Hsu, J. B. Dixon, Zhanna Nepiyushchikh  
**Venue/category:** Frontiers in Physiology  
**Date:** 2026-10-19  
**DOI:** `10.3389/fphys.2026.1912564`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** near-infrared fluorescence  

**Abstract**

Lymphedema is a chronic progressive disease resulting from impaired lymphatic transport. A wide range of animal models has been developed to study their pathophysiology, yet how well lymphatic function is measured within these models has received comparatively little attention. This mini-review organizes assessment methodologies into three complementary tiers: Tier 1 covers morphological and indirect outcome measures, including volumetric quantification and histological assessment; Tier 2 covers imaging-based approaches, including near-infrared fluorescence lymphangiography, radionuclide lymphoscintigraphy, and magnetic resonance lymphangiography; and Tier 3 covers direct physiological measurement of physical quantities such as intraluminal pressure, lymphatic conductance, and albumin transport rate. Surveying primary studies across seven species, we identify a recurring mismatch between the species most commonly used for lymphedema modeling and those in which the most informative measurements are technically accessible. The most accessible endpoints tend to reflect late-stage tissue consequences rather than early pumping dysfunction, imaging readouts remain constrained to relative dye intensity rather than true physical flow quantities, and direct physiological measurement is sparse and limited to single time points. For acquired secondary lymphedema, the rat hindlimb model paired with quantitative longitudinal imaging and vessel-level analysis offers a balanced platform, whereas genetic mouse models remain preferable for congenital disease and large-animal models for microsurgical and clinical-scale intervention studies; a cross-tabulation by species and measurement tier is provided to guide model and assessment selection.

### 387. [Spontaneous brain-wide rotational dynamics constrain cue-evoked behavior](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/16d0e8aab84d51442b92f1a9cacbc87e50d0d47a)

**Authors:** Si-Han Chen, Yun-Long Xu, Doruk Efe Gökmen, Michel Fruchart, B. Doiron, Vincenzo Vitelli  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-18  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.17.759884`  
**arXiv:** `2610.25061`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Uncovering low-dimensional representations of brain-wide neural dynamics and their relationship to behavior remains a central challenge. Here, we show that spontaneous brain-wide rotational dynamics constrain cue-evoked behavior and learning. To systematically establish these findings, we developed a dimensionality-reduction technique called Rotational Component Analysis (RCA) that optimizes the detection of low-variance rotational modes even within individual trials. We applied RCA to spike train data from motor cortices of monkeys and humans, mouse cortex-wide calcium imaging and in human functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI) and magnetoencephalography (MEG). In mice, a rotational mode identified from activity recorded at rest predicts cue-evoked behavior: the phase of rotation when a randomly timed cue arrives predicts whether the animal subsequently pulls the lever. We explain this surprisingly strong correlation using both a recurrent neural network (RNN) and a minimal two-dimensional model of response learning, in which the intrinsic rotational mode serves as a dynamical constraint on response and learning. We further identify double-rotation modes containing two centers of rotation that separate preparatory from task dynamics in both mouse and human data. Remarkably, corresponding modes also organize neural dynamics during a non-motor human task, and rotational irreversibility captures early psychosis even when a principal component analysis shows no group difference. Our work establishes rotational dynamics as low-dimensional coordinates that constrain how ongoing brain states are converted into behavior. More broadly, RCA provides a versatile tool for detecting hidden nonreciprocal dynamics in biological and physical datasets.

---

### 388. [Electron‐Transfer Engineered Biointerface With Detonation Nanodiamonds for Next‐Generation Cortisol Electrochemical Immunoassay](https://doi.org/10.1002/smll.76203)

**Authors:** Chakavak Esmaeili, Jaroslav Kuliček, Markéta Šlapal Bařinková, Bohuslav Rezek  
**Venue/category:** Small  
**Date:** 2026-10-18  
**DOI:** `10.1002/smll.76203`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** biointerface  

**Abstract**

ABSTRACT Detonation nanodiamonds (DND) emerge as versatile nano building blocks for next generation biosensing, owing to their exceptional physicochemical properties and biocompatibility. In this study, a robust label‐free electrochemical immunosensor for cortisol detection is based on screen printed carbon electrodes (SPE) modified with pristine DND. By utilizing EDC/NHS, efficient covalent crosslinking and stable antibody immobilization is achieved, as shown by comprehensive morphological and spectroscopic characterization (AFM, SEM, Raman, FTIR). Electrochemical analyses demonstrate that the DND interlayer functions as efficient multifunctional nanoscale mediator, enhancing interfacial electron transfer kinetics and providing stable biointerface. The resulting immunosensor exhibits high sensitivity with relevant linear range of 0.1 to 100 nM and detection limit of 10 pM, alongside high selectivity against structurally related hormones and robust long‐term storage stability. Validation in artificial saliva matrices demonstrates reliable performance and successful recovery. Benchmarking confirms that the DND‐engineered SPE can offer a scalable, low‐cost, reliable, competitive platform for non‐invasive cortisol monitoring in point‐of‐care applications.

### 389. [A cholinergic eligibility trace facilitates amygdala plasticity in flavour avoidance learning](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/aca3146558e2191ba8f9c60ee253f16449d8bf4f)

**Authors:** Weston Fleming, Jordan L. Pauli, Kentaro K. Ishii, Marta Trzeciak, Cassidy T. Burke, Sekun Park, Ruohe Liu, Adam G. Gordon, Larry S. Zweifel, Richard D. Palmiter, G. Stuber  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-16  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.07.02.734913`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

When an animal feels ill after consumption of a novel food, it rapidly learns to avoid this food in the future, a form of learning termed conditioned flavour avoidance (CFA). Postingestive malaise often occurs long after consumption, necessitating a neural mechanism that can facilitate plasticity between temporally distant events. Neuromodulators, acting through G-protein-coupled receptors (GPCRs) that can influence neuronal excitability on extended timescales, may facilitate this process. The projection of parabrachial (PB) Calca neurons to the central amygdala (CeA) promotes formation of CFA. Here, we demonstrate that these neurons overlap with a PB population that releases the neuromodulator acetylcholine (ACh) in the CeA during both consumption of a novel solution and subsequent visceral malaise, consistent with a role in CFA acquisition. Calcium imaging in brain slices reveals that ACh widely activates CeA neurons and enhances glutamatergic responsivity on a timescale consistent with CFA learning. CRISPR-Cas9-mediated genetic knockdown and optogenetics demonstrate that ACh from PB facilitates CFA acquisition. Loss of ACh signalling to CeA blocks key signatures of CFA-associated plasticity. Together, these data reveal that cholinergic input from PB to central amygdala is a critical neuromodulatory signal that links activity over long timespans to facilitate associative learning.

---

### 390. [The claustrum contributes to working memory maintenance in mice](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/fe966260e31babb0a9cb19c119aef4ecb672432e)

**Authors:** Anindya S. Bhattacharjee, Maxime Juventin, Chieko Huber, B. Ünsal, Jean-Rodolphe Renfer, Iván Rodríguez, A. Carleton  
**Venue/category:** Nature Communications  
**Date:** 2026-10-14  
**DOI:** `10.1038/s41467-026-78363-6`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Working memory (WM) enables the temporary maintenance and manipulation of sensory information to guide behavior, yet the neural circuits supporting this process remain incompletely defined. The claustrum is a highly interconnected brain structure positioned to integrate distributed cortical activity, but its contribution to WM has remained unclear. Here, we combined microendoscopic calcium imaging, chemogenetic and optogenetic manipulations in mice performing sensory-guided WM tasks to examine the role of the claustrum in WM. Claustrum neurons exhibited cue-evoked activity that persisted across delay periods and evolved dynamically over time, with population states retaining information about cue identity on single trials. Reducing claustrum excitability during encoding, delay maintenance, or target comparison similarly impaired WM performance across sensory modalities, without affecting sensory processing, motor output, arousal or inhibitory control. Together, these findings reveal an important causal contribution of the claustrum to WM maintenance.

### 391. [Targeted stimulation of motor cortex ensembles drives learned movements.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/520d69f75936091de23429ef95f9694d1eecb87b)

**Authors:** Qi-Yu Chen, An Wu, Bin Yu, S. Chae, Zi-Jing Tan, Assaf Ramot, Johnatan Aljadeff, T. Komiyama  
**Venue/category:** Nature  
**Date:** 2026-10-14  
**DOI:** `10.1038/s41586-026-11174-3`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

During the execution of learned motor skills, the neural population in layer 2/3 (L2/3) of primary motor cortex (M1) expresses a reproducible spatiotemporal activity pattern1. It is debated whether M1 actively participates in generating this learned pattern and whether this pattern causally drives the learned movement2-5. Here we combined in vivo two-photon calcium imaging and holographic optogenetic stimulation of functionally defined M1 L2/3 ensembles in mice performing a skilled lever-pressing task. Brief synchronous stimulation of about 20 neurons whose activity onset precedes voluntary movement onset induced movements resembling the learned movement, while producing activity patterns in other M1 neurons resembling the learned activity. Moreover, trial-by-trial variability of optogenetically triggered population activity correlated with variability in the induced movements. These variabilities were predicted by the initial state of M1 activity preceding stimulation. In some trials, stimulation induced the learned activity without inducing movements, indicating that the learned activity is not simply a reflection of movements but can arise internally. Stimulation failed to generate movements or learned activity in task-disengaged mice. Stimulation of neurons whose activity followed movement onsets failed to induce the learned movement in task-engaged mice. Together, the learned activity in M1 L2/3 can be generated when M1 is prepared at the optimal initial state and receives precise triggering inputs, supporting the active role of M1 in generating learned activity and learned movements.

---

### 392. [Nethobench: Forecasting design and conditioning shape the structural realism of generated neural dynamics](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/a77ad46127d092403579c948ddad9820e0cbe0b8)

**Authors:** Blanche Marion, Andrés De Vicente, Mathias Mahn, A. Lüthi, Leon Luithlen, C. Mitelut  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv

**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.07.757249`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Forecasting models are increasingly used to model neural population activity, yet existing benchmarks typically evaluate them by pointwise predictive fidelity to held-out observations. In stochastic, partially observed systems, agreement with one sampled realization is distinct from preserving the statistical and dynamical structure of the process. We introduce Nethobench, a benchmark that evaluates structural agreement across five metric families: distributional, temporal–spectral, relational, geometric, and state-dynamical. We validate Nethobench using targeted perturbations and stochastic systems with known dynamics, and apply it to mouse calcium imaging, mouse Neuropixels recordings, and human intracranial recordings, using empirical references to calibrate interpretation. Nethobench detects targeted structural failures and assigns high scores to independent samples from the correct synthetic process despite trajectory mismatch. It tracks structural changes during training and under domain shift. Model comparisons reveal how forecasting formulation, architecture, training regime, and loss shape structural profiles, while OmniMouse input ablations improve all five family means when behavior is added to video in one held-out session. In Brain Treebank, analog retrieval passes more structural checks while a multistep GRU better predicts the observed realization. These results motivate reporting calibrated family-level structural metrics alongside pointwise accuracy, proper probabilistic scores, and task-specific endpoints when evaluating neural forecasts across species and recording modalities.

### 393. [Dual-Mode Biointerface Engineering of Black Phosphorus via Electrostatic and Aptamer Recognition for Enhanced DNA Loading and Biosensing](https://doi.org/10.1021/acs.analchem.6c05292)

**Authors:** Yong Jian Jiang, Chun Mei Li, Cheng Zhi Huang, Jingtao Huang

**DOI:** `10.1021/acs.analchem.6c05292`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** biointerface  

**Abstract**

Abstract Black phosphorus (BP) holds significant promise as a DNA anchoring platform. However, its application is currently limited by inefficient single-stranded DNA (ssDNA) loading arising from electrostatic repulsion and surface corrugation. Herein, we present a dual-mode biointerface engineering strategy utilizing cytochrome c (Cyt c) to bridge the interface between BP and DNA. Unlike simple electrostatic adsorption, Cyt c enhances loading through a synergistic mechanism combining electrostatic attraction with specific protein-aptamer recognition. This dual-mode approach significantly improved the loading efficiency of a Cy5-modified Cyt c aptamer (Cy5-Cyt c Apt) from 21.7% to 66.5% compared to bare BP. The engineered BP/Cyt c interface facilitated effective static fluorescence quenching, enabling the construction of a proof-of-concept biosensing nanoplatform with enhanced capacity in buffer conditions. Mechanistic investigation using proteins with varying isoelectric points confirmed the critical role of specific recognition in this enhancement. This work establishes a simple, rapid, and effective model for constructing robust BP/DNA nanoplatforms with enhanced analytical performance.

### 394. [Arousal-informed neuronal avalanches operate near criticality](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/21504d0a14f27b1aecba065cd0923639b17756bb)

**Authors:** C. Stefens, Hardik Rajpal, Joseph S. Canzano, M. Saeedian, Michael G. Kareithi, H. J. Jensen, Mauricio Barahona, S. Smith, Simon R. Schultz  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-13  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.12.757995`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Cortical networks are thought to operate near a “critical point” that optimizes information processing, but this has been difficult to test at single-cell resolution across large brain areas, as narrow fields of view miss overlapping neural avalanches, causing subcritical estimates, Using mesoscopic two-photon calcium imaging in awake mouse visual cortex, we recorded thousands of neurons and applied convolutional non-negative matrix factorization to decompose population activity into distinct, overlapping spatiotemporal avalanches. Rather than occupying a single dynamical state, we find that the cortex hosts multiple overlapping subnetworks distributed across a phase diagram at varying distances from the critical point. Arousal-linked avalanches propagate widely and operate nearest the critical point, showing optimized dynamic range, minimized algorithmic complexity, and maximized temporal entropy. Our results reveal how the awake brain tunes parallel near-critical subnetworks to support robust sensory computation.

**Abstract**

Quality control of lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) is central to development of mRNA therapeutics, yet ensemble measurements obscure critical heterogeneity in particle morphology and mRNA cargo loading. Here we introduce PINPOINT (Profiling Individual Nanoparticles with Precise Observation of INternalized Therapeutics), a single-particle nanoscopy platform for non-destructive, multiparametric profiling of preformulated LNP samples. Super-resolution imaging with Nile Red resolves size and morphology, while an optimized permeabilization strategy provides selective access to encapsulated mRNA without compromising LNP integrity. Single-particle fluorescence measurements, including RiboGreen and sequence-specific probes, enable quantification and validation of mRNA loading and identity. Comparative analyses of bare and encapsulated mRNA reveal kinetic and structural features, exposing heterogeneity masked by bulk assays. By integrating structural and molecular readouts at the single-particle level, PINPOINT establishes a quantitative framework for assessing of LNP composition and payload distribution, offering actionable insights for improving batch consistency and guiding the rational design of next-generation mRNA delivery systems.

**Abstract**

Abstract Implant manufacturing routes determine surface architecture and, consequently, modulate biointerface interactions that govern early biological responses. Although additive manufacturing has emerged as a promising alternative to conventional subtractive techniques, direct comparisons among commercially available systems remain limited. In this study, a commercially available additively manufactured dental implant was compared with four market-available conventionally machined systems to elucidate manufacturing route-dependent biointerface modulation. Physicochemical properties, surface topography, and electrochemical behavior were evaluated alongside biological responses. Protein adsorption profiles were characterized by liquid chromatography–tandem mass spectrometry (LC–MS/MS), while polymicrobial accumulation was quantified and profiled. Microbially induced surface degradation was assessed by titanium ion release. The additively manufactured implant and the market-leading conventional system exhibited higher surface roughness and distinctive topographical features, which were associated with reduced polymicrobial biofilm accumulation, lower titanium ion release following microbial challenge, and stable electrochemical behavior. Although conventionally machined implants exhibited broader proteomic profiles, functional enrichment analyses revealed overlapping biological categories among implant systems. Collectively, the findings demonstrate that the manufacturing route critically modulates implant biointerface characteristics, with additive manufacturing achieving performance comparable to established commercial systems.

### 397. [Astrocytes in the Dentate Gyrus Shift the Equilibrium from Pattern Separation to Pattern Completion and Inhibit Fear Extinction](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/fc6fb72a93066efc6d6a2dab66fd6b5c52b2dd7b)

**Authors:** Mu-Xi Wang, Maltesh Kambali, Rajasekar Nagarajan, Jinrui Lyu, Hsun-Yi Kuan, Xin-Zhu Yu, Uwe Rudolph  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-12  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.05.755780`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Pattern separation and pattern completion are core hippocampal computations linked to memory interference and overgeneralization in many neuropsychiatric disorders and to mnemonic discrimination deficits in neurodegenerative conditions. Astrocytes are increasingly recognized as important integrators and modulators of neuronal activity as many studies have shown changes in animal behavior and neural circuits induced by manipulation of astrocytic activity. The role of astrocytes in the dentate gyrus for learning and memory is, however, largely unknown. To investigate the functional significance of astrocytic activity in the dentate gyrus, we expressed the calcium extruding pump hPMCA2w/b (CalEx), resulting in inhibition of astrocytic calcium activity, the Gq-coupled receptor hM3Dq, or the Gi-coupled receptor hM4Di, allowing chronic activation of Gq- and Gi-dependent pathways by a designer drug, in dentate gyrus astrocytes. Our main finding is that inhibition of astrocytic calcium activity by CalEx resulted in improved pattern separation and impaired pattern completion. In vivo calcium imaging revealed that CalEx expression in astrocytes altered the neuronal activity pattern in dentate gyrus neurons with an overall inhibitory effect. This suggests that in the dentate gyrus astrocytes promote the activity of these neurons. Furthermore, chronic activation of hM4Di resulted in impaired between-session fear extinction while chronic activation of hM3Dq resulted in impaired within-session fear extinction and improved pattern separation learning over multiple days, suggesting that activation of astrocytic activity impairs mechanistically separable forms of fear extinction in a Gq- or Gi-pathway-specific manner. Our findings reveal that dentate gyrus astrocytic calcium shifts the balance between pattern separation and pattern completion towards pattern completion and thus modulates an essential neuronal functional output of the dentate gyrus. Chronic activation of astrocytes inhibits fear extinction, and thus also affects management of memory interference. Our findings imply that astrocytic modulation may be beneficial to treat cognitive deficits in disorders in which overgeneralization is a problem.

### 398. [PD-L1-Targeted and ROS-Responsive Nanoplatform for Simultaneous Chemotherapy and Dual-Modal Imaging of Treatment Response in Triple-Negative Breast Cancer.](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/b4b5770896184c260490c9340259bbc08bc6d23d)

**Authors:** Jing-Jing Hu, Chun-Ting Wang, Yan-Hong Chen, Cui-Ying Zhu, Ao-Yu Liu, Dian Chen, Ling Zhan, De-Fan Yao, Deng-Bin Wang  
**Venue/category:** ACS Sensors

**DOI:** `10.1021/acssensors.6c03428`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** near-infrared fluorescence  

**Abstract**

The evaluation of treatment response in triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) still primarily relies on tumor size and ex vivo analyses, which provide very limited information on how tumors dynamically respond during therapy. In fact, oxidative stress rapidly emerges early after drug treatment, while PD-L1 reflects an adaptive signal associated with tumors. However, these treatment-associated features are commonly assessed using static detection methods, thereby limiting longitudinal in vivo evaluation. To address this, we developed a theranostic nanoprobe named RAPP, which integrates DOX delivery, ROS-responsive near-infrared fluorescence (NIRF) imaging, and PD-L1-targeted magnetic resonance imaging (MRI). In this system, the fluorescence signal serves to report early oxidative stress in real time, while PD-L1 targeting contributes to tumor-directed MRI, enabling longitudinal assessment of both signals in a 4T1 TNBC model. During treatment, RAPP produced distinct changes in fluorescence and MR signals across different experimental groups, indicating the complementarity of these two imaging modalities in evaluating tumor response. Furthermore, DOX-loaded RAPP effectively inhibited tumor growth without exhibiting significant systemic toxicity. By integrating drug delivery with dual-modal imaging, RAPP offers a practical strategy for assessing tumor response in TNBC during treatment, rather than only after treatment completion.

### 399. [Learning Interpretable Switching Dynamics in Shared Neural-Behavioral Latent Space](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/d661296e515643e3f65c17daf6732a1a9d38963e)

**Authors:** Yong-Xu Zhang, Josue Ortega Caro, Rachel Oren, M. Higley, Jessica A. Cardin, Anne K. Churchland, Shreya Saxena  
**Venue/category:** bioRxiv  
**Date:** 2026-10-09  
**DOI:** `10.64898/2026.10.02.756323`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 7  
**Matched:** calcium imaging  

**Abstract**

Modern recording technologies enable simultaneous measurement of high-dimensional neural activity and rich behavioral variables, creating both an opportunity and a modeling challenge: identifying the shared latent structure that mediates the brain-behavior relationship and the underlying dynamics through which it evolves across behavioral states. Existing approaches focus on different parts of this challenge: shared representation learning methods identify neural-behavioral subspaces but typically do not model dynamical structure, while dynamical models capture temporal variation and discrete state transitions in neural activity, but do not explicitly disentangle shared neural-behavioral structure from modality-specific variability. We introduce Switching Shared Latent Dynamics (SSLD), a unified framework that learns a latent representation shared between neural activity and behavior and endows this shared space with nonlinear switching recurrent dynamics to reflect the hypothesis that behaviorally relevant neural representations exhibit structured dynamical changes across behavioral states. Complementary private latent variables capture modality-specific variability, providing a window into neural activity that does not directly interact with behavior. We evaluate SSLD on a simulated dataset and four diverse experimental datasets spanning species, brain regions, and recording modalities: monkey motor and premotor cortex during reaching, somatosensory cortex during a bump task, widefield calcium imaging across mouse dorsal cortex during (a) self-initiated decision-making and (b) spontaneous movements. Across all four datasets, SSLD accurately reconstructs neural and behavioral signals, recovers discrete states in the dynamics that align with experimentally defined behavioral epochs, and isolates behaviorally-relevant neural information in the shared latent while preserving private neural variability. Ablations confirm that shared representation learning, behavioral supervision, and switching dynamics each contribute to performance. SSLD offers an interpretable approach to modeling shared neural-behavioral dynamics that complements ongoing efforts toward foundation models for neuroscience.

**Abstract**

The past several decades have witnessed great success in bacterial cellulose (BC) and its hydrogel composites in terms of both broad and in-depth basic research and potential biomedical applications. Inspired by the natural properties of microorganisms, in situ biosynthesis strategies are receiving increasing attention for the structural and functional design of BC-based hydrogels. This review discusses these strategies with emphasis on their fabrication processes and underlying mechanisms. Structure-property relationships governing BC-based hydrogels-molecular structure, nanofibrous architecture, biocompatibility, mechanical performance, and hydration behavior are examined to establish a process-structure-property framework. Biomedical applications including wound healing, drug delivery, osteogenic therapy, bioelectronic sensing, and cardiovascular repair are critically evaluated with quantitative comparison to conventional materials. Finally, challenges and future prospects in standardized characterization, scalable manufacturing, biosafety assessment, and clinical translation are discussed. This review provides a mechanism-oriented guide for developing BC-based hydrogels via in situ strategies for next-generation healthcare applications.

### 401. [Brain-inspired computing in a brain-inspired medium](https://doi.org/10.33540/3860)

**Authors:** Tim Maarten Kamsma  
**Date:** 2026-10-21  
**DOI:** `10.33540/3860`  
**Source(s):** Crossref  
**Keyword score:** 6  
**Matched:** iontronic  

**Abstract**

The brain is the most complex system in the known universe and remains in many ways unparalleled in its capacity for learning and general intelligence. Fascinatingly, the brain only requires a minuscule power budget of about 20 W, roughly the equivalent of a single light bulb and a mere fraction of what supercomputers use. This has motivated research into “neuromorphic” devices that replicate not just the brain’s learning rules, but also its hardware. Interestingly, when we look at this hardware of the brain, we find something remarkable. Instead of electrons flowing through metal(loid) wires, we find ions (dissolved salt) transported through water. In other words, the brain is not an electronic circuit, like our conventional computers, but an iontronic circuit, a circuit that relies on these ions in water. Inspired by the brain's architecture and “iontronic” substrate, I developed theories that explain how artificial ion channels can mimic the brain’s ion channels and synapses, the pores and connections neurons use to generate and process signals. With these theories I designed circuits that produce the voltage spikes our neurons use to send information, and, together with experimental collaborators, achieved the first demonstrations of iontronic brain-inspired computing. Moreover, leveraging the unique properties of ions in water, I showed how iontronic networks could be orders of magnitude more power-efficient at analysing specific natural signals. These results, and more, helped lay the foundations for a new computing technology that closely mimics our brain’s architecture and fluidic substrate, a technology that both walks and talks like the brain.

---

### 402. [Abstract C103: Precision MUC1-targeted nanomedicine reprograms oncogenic signaling and overcomes therapeutic resistance in racially diverse triple-negative breast cancer](https://www.semanticscholar.org/paper/dbda085c4f42329b88ae4701ff0ee1641973e04f)

**Authors:** Briana Kinnel, S. K. Singh, Rajesh Singh  
**Venue/category:** Cancer Epidemiology, Biomarkers & Prevention  
**Date:** 2026-10-18  
**DOI:** `10.1158/1538-7755.disp26-c103`  
**Source(s):** Semantic Scholar  
**Keyword score:** 6  
**Matched:** fluorescence imaging  

**Abstract**

Triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC) remains among the most lethal breast cancer subtypes. This disease disproportionately affects African American (AA) women, who develop earlier-onset, more aggressive basal-like tumors and experience significantly poorer survival compared to European American (EA) women. Despite these disparities, molecularly targeted therapies are limited. This study hypothesized that exploiting differential MUC1 expression through a precision nanomedicine platform could selectively eliminate aggressive TNBC while simultaneously targeting molecular pathways associated with therapeutic resistance and breast cancer. To test this hypothesis, MUC1-targeted planetary ball-milled nanoparticles (PBM-NPs) co-encapsulating paclitaxel (PTX) and honokiol (HNK) were engineered. Therapeutic efficacy was evaluated in racially representative TNBC models, including HCC70 (AA, Basal-like 2), MDA-MB-468 (AA, Basal-like 1), and MDA-MB-231 (EA, Claudin-low). MUC1 expression was quantified by western blotting, while antitumor activity was assessed using three-dimensional tumor spheroids, ATP-based viability assays, and live/dead fluorescence imaging. Mechanistic studies evaluated apoptosis, cell-cycle progression, proliferative signaling, and epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition (EMT) by immunoblotting, RT-PCR, and immunofluorescence analysis. Our unpublished findings revealed marked heterogeneity in MUC1 expression across TNBC subtypes, with AA-derived basal-like models exhibiting significantly higher MUC1 levels than the EA-derived claudin-low model. IC50 (PTX/PTX-NP, nM; HNK/HNK-NP, μM): HCC70 (6.94/5.82; 48.21/15.41), MDA-MB-468 (11.25/10.18; 49.73/13.02), and MDA-MB-231 (38.95/38.00; 38.99/38.03); combination doses (PTX-NP/HNK-NP) were 2.5/5, 2.5/5, and 10/5, respectively. These results showed MUC1-targeted PBM-NPs produced significantly greater inhibition of tumor spheroid growth and increased tumor cell death compared with free-drug controls and non-targeted nanoparticles. Mechanistically, treatment induced robust apoptosis, as evidenced by increased levels of cleaved caspase-3 and PARP, suppressed proliferative signaling through downregulation of phosphorylated AKT, STAT3, and NF-κB, reduced expression of the proliferation marker Ki-67, and inhibited EMT by decreasing N-cadherin and vimentin while restoring E-cadherin expression. These molecular effects were most pronounced in MUC1-high AA-derived TNBC models, identifying MUC1 as both a therapeutic target and a predictive biomarker of treatment response. Collectively, this work establishes a first-in-class precision nanotherapeutic strategy that integrates molecular targeting, combination drug delivery, and cancer health disparities into a single translational platform. By simultaneously suppressing multiple oncogenic pathways that drive proliferation, survival, and metastasis, MUC1-targeted PBM-NPs offer a promising therapeutic strategy for aggressive TNBC and provide a strong rationale for future clinical translation aimed at reducing breast cancer mortality and racial disparities. Briana Kinnel, Santosh K. Singh, Rajesh Singh. **Abstract**

Theoretical and numerical simulations reveal negative differential conductivity in DNA-based p⁺nn⁺ long structures operating under carriers’ double injection mode. The calculated impedance exhibits distinct resistive and reactive components whose frequency dependence enables transitions between capacitive and inductive behavior. The simulations also show that deep energy levels associated with sugar-phosphate groups surrounding the DNA play a key role in forming the region of negative differential conductance. These results suggest that DNA-based structures can function as nanoscale resistive and reactive elements in AC circuits. In particular, their impedance characteristics could enable the realization of molecular RC, RL, and RLC filters for frequency-selective signal processing. These findings establish a physical basis for the development of DNA-based functional elements for molecular electronics and bioelectronic circuits.
